Phenomenology of J P ​ C = 3 − ⁣ − J^{PC}=3^{--} tensor mesons Preprint: APS/123-QED
Shahriyar Jafarzade 1, Adrian Koenigstein 2, Francesco Giacosa 1,2

Abstract
We study the strong and radiative decays of the antiquark-quark ground state J P ​ C = 3 − ⁣ − J^{PC}=3^{--} ( n 2 ​ S + 1 ​ L J = 1 3 ​ D 3 n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{3}D_{3} ) nonet { ρ 3 ​ ( 1690 ) \rho_{3}(1690) , K 3 ∗ ​ ( 1780 ) K_{3}^{\ast}(1780) , ϕ 3 ​ ( 1850 ) \phi_{3}(1850) , ω 3 ​ ( 1670 ) \omega_{3}(1670) } in the framework of an effective quantum field theory approach, based on the S ​ U V ​ ( 3 ) SU_{\mathrm{V}}(3) flavor symmetry. The effective model is fitted to experimental data listed by the Particle Data Group . We predict numerous experimentally unknown decay widths and branching ratios. An overall agreement of theory (fit and predictions) with experimental data confirms the q ¯ ​ q \bar{q}q nature of the states and qualitatively validates the effective approach. Naturally, experimental clarification as well as advanced theoretical description is needed for trustworthy quantitative predictions, which is observed from some of the decay channels. Besides conventional spin- 3 3 mesons, theoretical predictions for ratios of strong and radiative decays of a hypothetical glueball state G 3 ​ ( 4200 ) G_{3}(4200) with J P ​ C = 3 − ⁣ − J^{PC}=3^{--} are also presented.



I
Introduction

The spectroscopy and the phenomenological description of conventional mesons is important for many reasons. It allows to test quantum chromodynamics (QCD) in the nonperturbative regime. Furthermore, it aims to the correct and systematic understanding and assignment of experimentally measured states, resonances and mesons
Zyla et al. 2020
. It is also fundamental for the search of mesons that go beyond the antiquark-quark (
\bar{q}q
) picture, such as glueballs, hybrids, and multiquark states. Namely, both in the light-quark and heavy-quark sectors, it is possible to search for “exotics” only, if the conventional
\bar{q}q
mesons are fully under control
Klempt and Zaitsev 2007
.

Consequently, our work is just an incremental contribution to this extensive project in high-energy physics. In our work, we provide a (qualitative) analysis of the phenomenology of the spin-
3
mesons
\rho_{3}(1690)
,
K_{3}^{\ast}(1780)
,
\phi_{3}(1850)
,
\omega_{3}(1670)
with
J^{PC}=3^{--}
(
n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{3}D_{3}
) as well as the potential spin-
3
tensor glueball
G_{3}(4200)

Morningstar and Peardon 1999
;
Meyer 2004
;
Chen et al. 2006
. This involves a fit of our effective model to experimental data as well as predictions for decay widths and ratios of the decays of the spin-
3
mesons. Although we provide explicit values for the decay widths and branching ratios, these should be considered only as first estimates and mainly interpreted as qualitative results. Hence, this study may for example help in identifying dominant decay channels
etc.
.

I.1
Contextualization

In the low-energy sector of QCD, conventional mesons contain the light up (
u
), down (
d
), and strange (
s
) quarks, hence they can be grouped into nonets:
\bar{u}d
,
\bar{u}s
,
etc.
. Nonets are classified by the quantum numbers
J^{PC}
, where
J
is the absolute value of the total spin of the meson (
|L-S|\leq J\leq|L+S|
, where
L
is the angular momentum and
S
the spin of the system),
P
is the sign-change under parity transformations, and
C
the sign-change under charge conjugation. For
\bar{q}q
states, besides
J^{PC}
, also the older spectroscopic notation
n^{2S+1}L_{J}
, with
L=S,P,D,\ldots
and with
n=1,2,\ldots
, which is the radial quantum number, is used. In the following, we shall restrict to the radial ground state nonets with
n=1
. For a
\bar{q}q
system, where
P=(-1)^{L+1}
and
C=(-1)^{L+S}
, notice that
L
and
S
are, strictly speaking, not well defined quantum numbers in a relativistic setup, hence mixing of states with the same
J^{PC}
but with different
L
and
S
is possible – even if it is oftentimes a subordinate effect. However, this nomenclature for the states is still well upheld also in presence of mixing for practical purposes.

In the low-energy regime of QCD the “classical” symmetries of QCD at high-energy scales still play a crucial role – more precisely the spontaneous, anomalous or explicit breaking of these symmetries. Quickly recapitulated, the situation is as follows: gluons are “democratic”: they interact equally strongly with right- and left-handed quarks implying that in the so-called chiral limit (
i.e.
assuming negligibly small bare quark masses
m_{u}
,
m_{d}
, and
m_{s}
) the QCD Lagrangian is invariant under
U_{\mathrm{L}}(3)\times U_{\mathrm{R}}(3)
transformations. However, this symmetry of the classical action is broken due to quantum effects, which is called the
U_{\mathrm{A}}(1)
-anomaly
Adler 1969
;
Bell and Jackiw 1969
;
’t Hooft 1976
;
Jackiw 2008
. Still, the baryon number is conserved, which corresponds to the unaffected
U_{\mathrm{V}}(1)
symmetry. The remaining
SU_{\mathrm{L}}(3)\times SU_{\mathrm{R}}(3)
symmetry is spontaneously broken at low energies into
SU_{\mathrm{V}}(3)

Peskin and Schroeder 1995
;
Weinberg 1996
. The
SU_{\mathrm{V}}(3)
amounts to a rotation in the flavor space spanned by
u
,
d
and
s
and is consequently called
flavor symmetry
or
vector symmetry
. Hence, at the composite mesonic level, chiral partner-mesons, which are linked through a chiral transformation, are not degenerate anymore. After all, the residual realization of an approximate
SU_{\mathrm{V}}(3)
, which is only explicitly broken by the bare quark masses, is the reason, why nonets of
\bar{q}q
states can be used to classify the low-energy spectrum of QCD
Gell-Mann 1961
;
Ne’eman 1961
;
Greiner and Müller 1994
.

Typical examples of very well-known mesonic nonets are the pseudoscalar states,

\displaystyle\{\pi,\,K,\,\eta^{\prime}(958),\,\eta\}\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(1)

with
J^{PC}=0^{-+}

(n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{1}S_{0})
, the vector mesons

\displaystyle\{\rho(770),\,K^{\ast}(892),\,\phi(1020),\,\omega(782)\}\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(2)

with
J^{PC}=1^{--}

(n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{3}S_{1})
, the pseudovector mesons,

\displaystyle\{b_{1}(1235),\,K_{1,B},\,h_{1}(1415),\,h_{1}(1170)\}\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(3)

with
J^{PC}=1^{+-}

(n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{1}P_{1})
, the axial-vector meson,

\displaystyle\{a_{1}(1260),\,K_{1,A},\,f_{1}(1420),\,f_{1}(1285)\}\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(4)

with
J^{PC}=1^{++}

(n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{3}P_{1})
, and the well-established tensor mesons,

\displaystyle\{a_{2}(1320),\,K_{2}^{\ast}(1430),\,f_{2}^{\prime}(1525),\,f_{2}(1270)\}\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(5)

with
J^{PC}=2^{++}

(n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{3}P_{2})
, compare with Ref.
Godfrey and Isgur 1985
or the
quark model review
by the
Particle Data Group
(PDG)
Zyla et al. 2020
.

The aim of this work is to study the
\bar{q}q
nonet with quantum numbers
J^{PC}=3^{--}
, resulting from
L=2
and
S=1
, therefore
n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{3}D_{3}
states, in a relativistic effective quantum field theory (QFT) model based on
SU_{\mathrm{V}}(3)
flavor symmetry. The mesons belonging to this nonet, corresponding to the lightest states with the proper quantum numbers in the PDG
Zyla et al. 2020
, are:

\displaystyle\{\rho_{3}(1690),\,K_{3}^{\ast}(1780),\,\phi_{3}(1850),\,\omega_{3}(1670)\}\,.

(6)

The study of these states is very interesting for a series of reasons (for previous studies on related subjects see also Refs.
Godfrey and Isgur 1985
;
Wang et al. 2017a
;
Wang et al. 2017b
;
Bergstrom et al. 1991
;
Harnad 1972
;
Pang et al. 2015
;
Caporale and Ivanov 2005
;
Johnson and Dudek 2020
and Refs. therein):

1.

It is the only well-known mesonic ground-state nonet with
J>2
, which is confirmed by several experiments
Zyla et al. 2020
.

2.

Many decay channels are known
Zyla et al. 2020
, which allows for further theoretical and experimental tests of the assignment.

3.

Predictions for not-yet measured strong and radiative decay rates are possible.

4.

The spin-
3
ground-state nonet (
6
) is also measured in lattice QCD calculations
Dudek et al. 2013
;
Johnson and Dudek 2020
.

This nonet is therefore tailor made for an effective QFT study of decays. In particular, we shall answer whether and to what extend the
\bar{q}q
assignment works, we can test validity of flavor symmetry for various decay channels, make various “postdictions” and – most interestingly – predictions for many decay channels. In view of the ongoing experimental efforts in hadron physics at
GlueX

Al Ghoul et al. 2016
;
Zihlmann 2010
;
Shepherd 2014
and
CLAS12

Rizzo 2016
at
Jefferson Lab
, at
COMPASS
and
LHCb
at
CERN

Ryabchikov 2019
;
Aaij et al. 2019
, at
BESIII
in Beijing
Mezzadri 2015
;
Marcello 2016
, and at the future
PANDA
experiment
Lutz et al. 2009
at the
GSI
facility, we consider a revival of interest on such resonances valuable.

As an additional application of our approach, we also study some decays of the
J^{PC}=3^{--}
glueball state. This can be done by a simple modification of the action of spin-
3
mesons and by using the mass of approximately
4.2\,\,
GeV found in lattice simulations
Morningstar and Peardon 1999
;
Meyer 2004
;
Chen et al. 2006
(in the quenched approximation) as an input. Due to the flavor-singlet nature of the glueball, only few decay channels are possible.

I.2
Method

From a technical point of view, we construct
SU_{\mathrm{V}}(3)
-invariant effective actions/Lagrangians that involve the mesonic nonet (
6
) as well as its various decay products consisting in the well-established
\bar{q}q
nonets that were previously introduced. From a (Functional) Renormalization Group (FRG) perspective, the effective actions/Lagrangians can be interpreted as residual infrared (IR) effective actions with coupling constants that already involve all quantum effects from higher energy scales, the ultraviolet (UV). These coupling constants are determined via fits to experimental data, rather than by ab initio QCD-calculations. All calculations for the decays are therefore performed at tree-level. This approach was already implemented in earlier works for studies of various light mesonic nonets, such as tensor mesons
Giacosa et al. 2005a
, axial-vector, and pseudovector mesons
Divotgey et al. 2013
, the scalar mesons
Giacosa et al. 2005b
;
Giacosa et al. 2005c
, the pseudotensor mesons
Koenigstein and Giacosa 2016
, and the orbitally and radially excited vector mesons
Piotrowska et al. 2017
.

I.3
Organization and structure

We organize the paper as follows: in Chap.
II
, after a discussion of our low-energy effective model, we present antiquark-quark nonets, their transformation rules, and the corresponding Lagrangian of the model. It consists of seven interaction terms. In Chap.
III
we show the results for the decay widths and branching ratios of spin-
3
mesons, while in Chap.
IV
we list some branching ratios for an hypothetical heavy
3^{--}
glueball. A discussion of the validity of the employed effective interaction terms is reported in Chap.
V
and conclusions can be found in Chap.
VI
. Many technical aspects concerning the QFT treatment of
J=3
fields can be found in the numerous and detailed appendices.

II
Effective model for
J^{PC}=3^{--}
mesons

As mentioned in the introduction, in the chiral limit (
m_{u,d,s}=0
) the
SU_{\mathrm{L}}(3)\times SU_{\mathrm{R}}(3)\times U_{\mathrm{V}}(1)
symmetry of QCD is spontaneously broken into flavor symmetry
SU_{\mathrm{V}}(3)
and baryon-number conservation – the
U_{\mathrm{V}}(1)
symmetry. One of the first successful models that describes this process using four-Fermi interactions is given by Refs.
Nambu and Jona-Lasinio 1961a
;
Nambu and Jona-Lasinio 1961b
. At the level of confined light hadrons, this symmetry is evident: quark-antiquark mesons are clearly grouped into nonets, some of which were already listed in the introduction
Gell-Mann 1961
;
Ne’eman 1961
;
Greiner and Müller 1994
. One possibility to describe low-energy QCD makes use of effective low-energy hadronic models. If the degrees of freedoms are only hadrons, then confinement and color-neutrality is automatically built in. Typically, these models are defined by a proper action that mimics the chiral symmetry of QCD and its spontaneous and explicit breaking.

The chiral symmetry is for example the guiding principle of chiral perturbation theory (ChPT)
Gasser and Leutwyler 1984
;
Gasser and Leutwyler 1985
;
Scherer 2003
;
Leutwyler 1994
;
Pich 1995
;
Bernard and Meissner 2007
;
Schindler et al. 2005
;
Jenkins et al. 1995
;
Booth et al. 1997
;
Terschlüsen and Leupold 2016
;
Cirigliano et al. 2003
, in which chiral symmetry is nonlinearly realized, as well as for linear
\sigma
-models
Gell-Mann and Levy 1960
;
Parganlija et al. 2013
;
Ko and Rudaz 1994
;
Carter et al. 1996
;
Janowski et al. 2014
;
Koch 1997
, in which it is linearly realized. In effective models the nonets usually appear in pairs of chiral partners. The breaking of chiral symmetry generates a mass difference between them. This dynamics can also be studied at nonzero temperature and/or nonzero baryon or isospin chemical potentials. Within the last years, the Functional Renormalization Group
Gies 2012
;
Polonyi 2003
;
Wetterich 1993
;
Morris 1994
;
Dupuis et al. 2020
;
Pawlowski 2007
, turned out to be one particularly interesting framework to describe chiral symmetry breaking (and phase transitions) in these kind of models, see for example Refs.
Grahl and Rischke 2013
;
Eser et al. 2015
;
Rennecke and Schaefer 2017
;
Pawlowski and Rennecke 2014
;
Eser et al. 2018
;
Divotgey et al. 2019
;
Eser et al. 2020
;
Cichutek et al. 2020
;
Otto et al. 2020a
;
Otto et al. 2020b
;
Jung and von
Smekal 2019
;
Heller and Mitter 2016
.

Hadronic models in which only the residual flavor symmetry
SU_{\mathrm{V}}(3)
is explicitly conserved have also been constructed for a variety of nonets in a series of publications by one of the authors and collaborators
Giacosa et al. 2005b
;
Giacosa et al. 2005c
;
Piotrowska et al. 2017
;
Divotgey et al. 2013
;
Giacosa et al. 2005a
;
Koenigstein and Giacosa 2016
. These models can be interpreted as the effective emerging terms of chiral models after spontaneous symmetry breaking is worked out. When the effective action is written down, only flavor symmetry is retained and an expansion in dominant and subdominant terms in
1/N_{\mathrm{c}}
is carried out. Moreover, terms that break explicitly flavor symmetry, either because of the underlying breaking due to nonzero and unequal quark masses (
m_{u}\approx 2\,\,
MeV,
m_{d}\approx 5\,\,
MeV and
m_{s}\approx 93\,\,
MeV) or due to the chiral anomaly can be included.

In this work, we study the decays of the
\bar{q}q
ground-state
J^{PC}=3^{--}
nonet by constructing such a model. We shall consider only the dominant terms in the large-
N_{\mathrm{c}}
expansion
’t Hooft 1974
;
Witten 1979
;
Lebed 1999
and neglect flavor symmetry breaking corrections, since the present level of data accuracy does not allow for their investigations.

II.1
Particle content

II.1.1
Symmetries

Mesonic nonets that transform under the adjoint representation of the approximate flavor symmetry
SU_{\mathrm{V}}(3)
are the main ingredients of the current work. Transformation rules under parity, charge conjugation and flavor transformations of the mesonic nonets of our model are summarized

;

.
in the Table
1
. On the other hand, the mesonic nonets are in direct correspondence to the physical states, which is listed in Table
2
.

Each
\bar{q}q
nonet can be assigned to a certain microscopic antiquark-quark current
N_{ij}\equiv(\bar{q}_{j}\,\Gamma\,q_{i})/\sqrt{2}
, where
\Gamma
is a combination of Dirac matrices and derivatives, which – in the nonrelativistic limit – reduces to the corresponding nonrelativistic configuration with the desired
L
and
S
.

II.1.2
Mesonic nonets in the model

Pseudoscalar mesons

The first nonet of Table
1
describes the matrix of pseudoscalar mesons
P
with quantum numbers
L=S=0
, leading to
J^{PC}=0^{-+}

(n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{1}S_{0})
. The elements are
P_{ij}\equiv(\bar{q}_{j}\,\mathrm{i}\gamma^{5}\,q_{i})/\sqrt{2}
and the mesons are {
\pi
,
K
,
\eta^{\prime}(958)
,
\eta
}, where the first entry represents the isospin
I=1
triplet (the three pions), the second entry the two
I=1/2
isodoublets (the four kaons), and the last two entries the two isoscalar states (the etas). Pseudoscalars form the basis of almost all low-energy effective hadronic models/limits or theories of QCD,
e.g.
ChPT
Gasser and Leutwyler 1984
;
Gasser and Leutwyler 1985
;
Scherer 2003
;
Leutwyler 1994
;
Pich 1995
;
Bernard and Meissner 2007
;
Schindler et al. 2005
;
Jenkins et al. 1995
;
Booth et al. 1997
;
Terschlüsen and Leupold 2016
and
\sigma
-models
Gell-Mann and Levy 1960
;
Ko and Rudaz 1994
;
Carter et al. 1996
;
Parganlija et al. 2013
;
Koch 1997
(for an link between ChPT and
\sigma
-models, see Ref.
Divotgey et al. 2018
;
Eser et al. 2018
;
Divotgey et al. 2019
;
Cichutek et al. 2020
). Explicitly, the matrix
P
reads:

\displaystyle P=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\eta_{N}+\pi^{0}}{\sqrt{2}}&\pi^{+}&K^{+}\\
\pi^{-}&\frac{\eta_{N}-\pi^{0}}{\sqrt{2}}&K^{0}\\
K^{-}&\bar{K}^{0}&\eta_{S}\end{pmatrix}\,,

(7)

where
\eta_{N}\equiv(\bar{u}\,\mathrm{i}\gamma^{5}\,u+\bar{d}\,\mathrm{i}\gamma^{5}\,d)/\sqrt{2}
stands for the the purely nonstrange state and
\eta_{S}\equiv\bar{s}\,\mathrm{i}\gamma^{5}\,s
stands for the pure strange state. For the pions and kaons, the physical states are directly assigned to the fields in the model. In the isoscalar sector, physical and model fields are related by mixing
2
2

2

We refer to App.
A
for the description of the mass terms of the corresponding Lagrangian(s) and for the derivation of the PDG-mixing formula, which was applied in the PDG
Zyla et al. 2020
in the case of ground-state vector and pseudoscalar mesons as well as for the ground-state tensor mesons with
J^{PC}=2^{++}
and
J^{PC}=3^{--}
. We shall also present the link between the singlet-octet basis used in the PDG and the strange-nonstrange one employed in this work.
,

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\eta\\
\eta^{\prime}(958)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\beta_{p}&\sin\beta_{p}\\
-\sin\beta_{p}&\cos\beta_{p}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\eta_{N}\\
\eta_{S}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(8)

Here we shall use
\beta_{p}=-43.4^{\circ}
obtained in Ref.
Amelino-Camelia et al. 2010
. Note, the rather large mixing angle and the unexpectedly high mass of the
\eta^{\prime}(958)
result from the chiral (or axial) anomaly
U_{\mathrm{A}}(1)

Feldmann et al. 1998
;
’t Hooft 1986
. According to a classifications introduced by two of the authors of the present work in Ref.
Giacosa et al. 2018
, pseudoscalar mesons together with their chiral partners (the scalars), belong to what we call a “heterochiral multiplet”, which allows for the constructions of chirally anomalous mass and interaction terms.

Vector mesons

Next, the second entry in Table
1
refers to the
J^{PC}=1^{--}

(n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{3}S_{1})
nonet with
L=0
and
S=1
. These are the very well-known vector states {
\rho(770)
,
K^{\ast}(892)
,
\omega(782)
,
\phi(1020)
}, which are for example also included in certain extensions of ChPT
Schindler et al. 2005
;
Jenkins et al. 1995
;
Booth et al. 1997
;
Terschlüsen and Leupold 2016
as well as in enlarged (and realistic) versions of hadronic models
Gell-Mann and Levy 1960
;
Parganlija et al. 2013
;
Ko and Rudaz 1994
;
Carter et al. 1996
;
Janowski et al. 2014
. The matrix
V_{1}^{\mu}
with elements
V_{1,ij}^{\mu}=(\bar{q}_{j}\,\gamma^{\mu}\,q_{i})/\sqrt{2}
has the form

\displaystyle V_{1}^{\mu}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\omega_{1,N}^{\mu}+\rho_{1}^{0\mu}}{\sqrt{2}}&\rho_{1}^{+\mu}&K_{1}^{\ast+\mu}\\
\rho_{1}^{-\mu}&\frac{\omega_{1,N}^{\mu}-\rho_{1}^{0\mu}}{\sqrt{2}}&K_{1}^{\ast 0\mu}\\
K_{1}^{\ast-\mu}&\bar{K}_{1}^{\ast 0\mu}&\omega_{1,S}^{\mu}\end{pmatrix}\,,

(9)

where
\omega_{N}
and
\omega_{S}
are purely nonstrange and strange states, respectively. Similarly as before, the physical fields arise upon mixing

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\omega(782)\\
\phi(1020)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\beta_{v_{1}}&\sin\beta_{v_{1}}\\
-\sin\beta_{v_{1}}&\cos\beta_{v_{1}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\omega_{1,N}\\
\omega_{1,S}\end{pmatrix}\,,

(10)

where the very small isoscalar-vector mixing angle
\beta_{v_{1}}=-3.9^{\circ}
is taken from the PDG
Zyla et al. 2020
. Hence, the physical states
\omega(782)
and
\phi(1020)
are dominated by nonstrange and strange components, respectively. This is in agreement with the “homochiral” nature of these states
Giacosa et al. 2018
. In fact vector and axial-vector mesons form a “homochiral multiplet”, for which the effect of the chiral anomaly is of subleading order and can safely be ignored.

Pseudovector mesons

Next, we consider
L=1
. The choice
L=1
and
S=0
, leading to
J^{PC}=1^{+-}

(n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{1}P_{1})
, contains the established pseudovector mesons {
b_{1}(1235)
,
K_{1,B}
,
h_{1}(1170)
,
h_{1}(1415)
}. The corresponding nonet with elements
B_{1,ij}^{\mu}=(\bar{q}_{j}\,\gamma^{5}\,\partial^{\mu}\,q_{i})/\sqrt{2}
is

\displaystyle B_{1}^{\mu}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{h_{1,N}^{\mu}+b_{1}^{0\mu}}{\sqrt{2}}&b_{1}^{+\mu}&K_{1,B}^{+\mu}\\
b_{1}^{-\mu}&\frac{h_{1,N}^{\mu}-b_{1}^{0\mu}}{\sqrt{2}}&K_{1,B}^{0\mu}\\
K_{1,B}^{-\mu}&\bar{K}_{1,B}^{0\mu}&h_{1,S}^{\mu}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(11)

The mixing angle

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}h_{1}(1170)\\
h_{1}(1415)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\beta_{b_{1}}&\sin\beta_{b_{1}}\\
-\sin\beta_{b_{1}}&\cos\beta_{b_{1}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}h_{1,N}\\
h_{1,S}\end{pmatrix}

(12)

is not known. Quite interestingly, since this nonet belongs to a heterochiral multiplet (the chiral partners are the orbitally excited vector mesons) the mixing angle could be nonnegligible, just as for pseudoscalar mesons. Here, we shall consider two scenarios for our model calculations: in the first, the mixing angle
\beta_{b_{1}}
is set to zero, in the second we consider a large mixing similar to the pseudoscalar sector,
\beta_{b_{1}}\approx-40^{\circ}
and similar to what two of the authors found for the pseudotensor meson nonet in Ref.
Koenigstein and Giacosa 2016
and elaborated on in Ref.
Giacosa et al. 2018
.

Tensor mesons

For
L=S=1
three nonets are possible. The well-known
J^{PC}=2^{++}

(n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{3}P_{2})
tensor states {
a_{2}(1320)
,
K_{2}^{\ast}(1430)
,
f_{2}(1270)
,
f_{2}^{\prime}(1525)
}, with elements
A_{2,ij}^{\mu}=[\bar{q}_{j}\,(\mathrm{i}\gamma^{\mu}\,\partial^{\nu}+\ldots)\,q_{i}]/\sqrt{2}
, represent an almost ideal nonet of quark-antiquark states

\displaystyle A_{2}^{\mu\nu}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{f_{2,N}^{\mu\nu}+a_{2}^{0\mu\nu}}{\sqrt{2}}&a_{2}^{+\mu\nu}&K_{2}^{\ast+\mu\nu}\\
a_{2}^{-\mu\nu}&\frac{f_{2,N}^{\mu\nu}-a_{2}^{0\mu\nu}}{\sqrt{2}}&K_{2}^{\ast 0\mu\nu}\\
K_{2}^{\ast-\mu\nu}&\bar{K}_{2}^{\ast 0\mu\nu}&f_{2,S}^{\mu\nu}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(13)

The physical isoscalar-tensor states are

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}f_{2}(1270)\\
f_{2}^{\prime}(1525)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\beta_{a_{2}}&\sin\beta_{a_{2}}\\
-\sin\beta_{a_{2}}&\cos\beta_{a_{2}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}f_{2,N}\\
f_{2,S}\end{pmatrix}\,,

(14)

where
\beta_{a_{2}}=5.7^{\circ}
is the small mixing angle reported in the PDG
Zyla et al. 2020
, in agreement with the fact that tensor mesons belong to a homochiral multiplet. The decays of tensor mesons were studied in great detail in Refs.
Giacosa et al. 2005a
;
Burakovsky and Goldman 1998
and fit very well into the
\bar{q}q
picture
Godfrey and Isgur 1985
.

Axial-vector mesons

The choice
L=S=1
allows also for the
J^{PC}=1^{++}

(n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{3}P_{1})
axial-vector nonet
A_{1}
, which contains the resonances {
a_{1}(1260)
,
K_{1,A}
,
f_{1}(1285)
,
f_{1}(1420)
} which are linked to the vector mesons mentioned above by chiral transformations, see
e.g.
Refs.
Hatanaka and Yang 2008
;
Cheng 2012
(building a homochiral multiplet). The nonet matrix, whose microscopic currents are
A_{1,ij}^{\mu}\equiv\bar{q}_{j}\,\gamma^{5}\gamma^{\mu}\,q_{i}/\sqrt{2}
, reads:

\displaystyle A_{1}^{\mu}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{f_{1,N}^{\mu}+a_{1}^{0\mu}}{\sqrt{2}}&a_{1}^{+\mu}&K_{1,A}^{+\mu}\\
a_{1}^{-\mu}&\frac{f_{1,N}^{\mu}-a_{1}^{0\mu}}{\sqrt{2}}&K_{1,A}^{0\mu}\\
K_{1,A}^{-\mu}&\bar{K}_{1,A}^{0\mu}&f_{1,S}^{\mu}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(15)

For the isoscalar sector we find,

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}f_{1}(1285)\\
f_{1}(1420)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\beta_{a_{1}}&\sin\beta_{a_{1}}\\
-\sin\beta_{a_{1}}&\cos\beta_{a_{1}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}f_{1,N}\\
f_{1,S}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(16)

The mixing angle
\beta_{a_{1}}
is expected to be small, as the homochiral nature of the multiplet
Giacosa et al. 2018
and the decay properties
Divotgey et al. 2013
suggest. We set
\beta_{a_{1}}=0
for simplicity (an anyhow small mixing angle would not affect our results very much).

It is important to note at this point, that the kaonic states
K_{1,A}
and
K_{1,B}
mix. For a study of the axial-vector and pseudovector nonets, with focus on the
K_{1}(1270)/K_{1}(1400)
system, by using an approach similar to the one used in this work, see Ref.
Divotgey et al. 2013
. The main result concerning the kaonic mixing reads:

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}K_{1}(1270)\\
K_{1}(1400)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\varphi_{K}&-\mathrm{i}\sin\varphi_{K}\\
-\mathrm{i}\sin\varphi_{K}&\cos\varphi_{K}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}K_{1,A}\\
K_{1,B}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(17)

Equivalently, in terms of kets and the angle
\theta_{K}=\varphi_{K}+90^{\circ}
typically used in the literature
Yang 2007
;
Hatanaka and Yang 2008
;
Cheng 2012
:

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}|K_{1}^{+}(1270)\rangle\\
|K_{1}^{+}(1400)\rangle\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\sin\theta_{K}&\cos\theta_{K}\\
\cos\theta_{K}&-\sin\theta_{K}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}|K_{1,A}^{+}\rangle\\
|K_{1,B}^{+}\rangle\end{pmatrix}\,.

(18)

The final result reads
\varphi_{K}=(56.4\pm 4.3)^{\circ}
, or equivalently
\theta_{K}=(-33.6\pm 4.3)^{\circ}
, hence
K_{1}(1270)
is predominantly
K_{1,B}
and
K_{1}(1400)
predominantly
K_{1,A}
, yet the mixing is still large.

Scalar mesons

For completeness, we recall that
L=S=1
yields also the scalar states
J^{PC}=0^{++}

(n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{3}P_{0})
, corresponding to the chiral partners of the pseudoscalar mesons. The scalars are subject to an ongoing controversial debate on the correct assignment of measured states. The identification is still uncertain, but the set

\displaystyle\{a_{0}(1450),K_{0}^{\ast}(1430),f_{0}(1710),f_{0}(1370)\}\,,

(19)

seems to be favored (for the description of mixing with
e.g.
the long-searched scalar glueball, see
e.g.
Refs.
Giacosa et al. 2005b
;
Giacosa et al. 2005c
;
Amsler and Close 1995
;
Amsler and Close 1996
;
Strohmeier-Presicek et al. 1999
;
Lee and Weingarten 1999
;
Cheng et al. 2006
;
Gutsche et al. 2016
and Refs. therein). The reason why this nonet is so peculiar is due to the fact that it has the quantum numbers of the QCD vacuum, hence condensation of the scalar-isoscalar fields should take place. The corresponding nonet
S
, whose microscopic currents are
S_{ij}\equiv(\bar{q}_{j}\,q_{i})/\sqrt{2}
, is not included in this work, since no decays of spin-
3
states into scalars can be realized in our model approach, see Sub.Sec.
II.2.3
.

Moreover, light scalar four-quark states (in the form and combination of tetraquark/molecular/companion poles) below
1\,\,
GeV also exist
Zyla et al. 2020
. Nevertheless, they are also not included our work.

Higher-spin mesons

Besides the peculiar case of the scalar nonet, all other nonets mentioned above are well-established. Yet, what about nonets with
L=2
? For
L=2
four nonets can be constructed, one of which is the main subject of this work. Thus, even if, besides spin-
3
fields, they do not enter into the Lagrangian of this work, since they are too heavy, they allow us to put the nonet of mesons with
J=3
into the correct physical framework. Hence, we briefly discuss them.

The easiest choice is
L=2
and
S=0,
which implies
J^{PC}=2^{-+}

(n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{1}D_{2})
: these so-called pseudotensor states with currents
[\bar{q}_{j}\,(\mathrm{i}\gamma^{5}\,\partial^{\mu}\partial^{\nu}+\ldots)\,q_{i}]/\sqrt{2}
are assigned to {
\pi_{2}(1670)
,
K_{2}(1770)
,
\eta_{2}^{\prime}(1870)
,
\eta_{2}(1645)
}
Wang et al. 2015
;
Chen et al. 2013
;
Kuhn et al. 2004
;
Koenigstein and Giacosa 2016
. Moreover, as recently described in Ref.
Giacosa et al. 2018
, the chiral anomaly may also be important for this nonet, explaining a potential large mixing between strange and nonstrange components, leading to the isoscalar states
\eta_{2}(1650)
and
\eta_{2}^{\prime}(1870)
. The details of the need for a large mixing angle can be found in Ref.
Koenigstein and Giacosa 2016
, in which strong decays of these mesons are studied in a similar relativistic effective model.

For
L=2
and
S=1
one can construct the orbitally excited vector
J^{PC}=1^{--}
(
n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{3}D_{1}
) states {
\rho(1700)
,
K^{\ast}(1680)
,
\phi(????)
,
\omega(1650)
}, which are the (hetero) chiral partners of the pseudovector mesons
Giacosa et al. 2017
;
Giacosa et al. 2018
. Microscopically, the elements are
(\bar{q}_{j}\mathrm{i}\partial^{\mu}q_{i})/\sqrt{2}
. The predominately strange member of the nonet
\phi(????)
could not be identified yet. For predictions of a possible
\phi(1930)
meson see Ref.
Piotrowska et al. 2017
and Refs. therein, in which a flavor-symmetry-based QFT model is employed for two nonets of excited vector mesons.

The quantum numbers
L=2
and
S=1
may also lead to a axial tensor nonet with
J^{PC}=2^{--}

(n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{3}D_{2})
with currents
[\bar{q}_{j}\,(\gamma^{5}\,\gamma^{\mu}\,\partial^{\nu}+\ldots)\,q_{i}]/\sqrt{2}
, which is however poorly understood. In fact, only the state
K_{2}(1820)
is identified and listed by the PDG
Zyla et al. 2020
. This nonet builds together with the
2^{++}
tensor mesons a homochiral multiplet.

The latter two nonets are omitted in this work, because their members are too heavy.

Spin-3 mesons

Finally and most importantly, the choice
L=2
and
S=1
can also lead the nonet of
J^{PC}=3^{--}

(n^{2S+1}L_{J}=1^{3}D_{3})
states {
\rho_{3}(1690)
,
K_{3}^{\ast}(1780)
,
\phi_{3}(1850)
,
\omega_{3}(1670)
}. The nonet with elements
W_{ij}^{\mu\nu\rho}=[\bar{q}_{j}\,(\gamma^{\mu}\,\partial^{\nu}\partial^{\rho}+\ldots)\,q_{i}]/\sqrt{2}
reads,

\displaystyle W_{3}^{\mu\nu\rho}=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\omega_{3,N}^{\mu\nu\rho}+\rho_{3}^{0\mu\nu\rho}}{\sqrt{2}}&\rho_{3}^{+\mu\nu\rho}&K_{3}^{+\mu\nu\rho}\\
\rho_{3}^{-\mu\nu\rho}&\frac{\omega_{3,N}^{\mu\nu\rho}-\rho_{3}^{0\mu\nu\rho}}{\sqrt{2}}&K_{3}^{0\mu\nu\rho}\\
K_{3}^{-\mu\nu\rho}&\bar{K}_{3}^{0\mu\nu\rho}&\omega_{3,S}^{\mu\nu\rho}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(20)

In this case the mixing angle

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\omega_{3}(1670)\\
\phi_{3}(1850)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\beta_{w_{3}}&\sin\beta_{w_{3}}\\
-\sin\beta_{w_{3}}&\cos\beta_{w_{3}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\omega_{3,N}\\
\omega_{3,S}\end{pmatrix}

(21)

is
\beta_{w_{3}}=3.5^{\circ}

Zyla et al. 2020
. The small mixing is also in agreement with the homochiral nature of the corresponding chiral multiplet in which this nonet is embedded
Giacosa et al. 2018
. In the next section we couple this nonet to the other nonets listed in Table
1
.

Zyla et al. 2020

II.2
Effective mesonic interactions

In this section, we present the effective mesonic interactions and discuss the derivation of the tree level decay widths from our model.

II.2.1
The effective action

Using the nonets introduced in the previous subsection, we construct the effective Lagrangian describing the strong decays of spin-
3
tensor mesons as follows,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{W,\text{total}}=\,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\text{mass}}+\mathcal{L}_{\text{kin}}+\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}pp}+\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}v_{1}p}+\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(22)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}a_{2}p}+\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}a_{1}p}+\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}b_{1}p}+\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}v_{1}v_{1}}\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

where
\mathcal{L}_{\text{kin}}=\frac{1}{2}\,\mathrm{tr}\,(\partial_{\mu}P)^{2}+\ldots
contains the usual kinetic terms and
\mathcal{L}_{\text{mass}}
contains all the quadratic mass terms describing the masses of all relevant nonets. In this work, all masses are taken from the PDG
Zyla et al. 2020
and assumed to be exact, while some mixing angles are derived from the masses following the
quark model review
of the PDG
Zyla et al. 2020
, see also App.
A
. The other terms describing the decays are listed explicitly in Table
3
. The quantities
g_{w_{3}\circ\circ}
are the coupling constants, which are fitted to experimental results,
\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
is the antisymmetric Levi-Civita pseudotensor,
[\circ,\,\circ]_{-}
stands for the commutator, and
\{\circ,\,\circ\}_{+}
for the anticommutator.

Table 3:
Effective relativistic interaction terms describing the strong decays of mesons with
J^{PC}=3^{--}
.

decay mode

interaction Lagrangians

3^{--}\rightarrow 0^{-+}+0^{-+}

\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}pp}=g_{w_{3}pp}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[W_{3}^{\mu\nu\rho}\,\big[P\,,(\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\rho}P)\big]_{-}\big]

3^{--}\rightarrow 0^{-+}+1^{--}

\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}v_{1}p}=g_{w_{3}v_{1}p}\,\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[W_{3,\mu\alpha\beta}\,\big\{(\partial_{\nu}V_{1,\rho})\,,(\partial^{\alpha}\partial^{\beta}\partial_{\sigma}P)\big\}_{+}\big]

3^{--}\rightarrow 0^{-+}+2^{++}

\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}a_{2}p}=g_{w_{3}a_{2}p}\,\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[\mathchoice{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{3}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}{\beta}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{3}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}{\beta}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{3}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}{\beta}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{3}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}{\beta}}}}\big[(\partial^{\nu}A_{2}^{\rho\alpha})\,,(\partial^{\sigma}\partial^{\beta}P)\big]_{-}\big]

\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}b_{1}p}=g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[W_{3}^{\mu\nu\rho}\big\{B_{1,\mu}\,,(\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\rho}P)\big\}_{+}\big]

3^{--}\rightarrow 0^{-+}+1^{++}

\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}a_{1}p}=g_{w_{3}a_{1}p}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[W_{3}^{\mu\nu\rho}\big[A_{1,\mu}\,,(\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\rho}P)\big]_{-}\big]

3^{--}\rightarrow 1^{--}+1^{--}

\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}v_{1}v_{1}}=g_{w_{3}v_{1}v_{1}}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[W_{3}^{\mu\nu\rho}\big[(\partial_{\mu}V_{1,\nu})\,,V_{1,\rho}\big]_{-}\big]

All the interaction Lagrangians in Table
3
are invariant under
CPT
- , Poincaré- and flavor transformations, listed in Table
1
. We considered only couplings involving a minimal number of derivatives within each single interaction term. This strategy turned to be successful in previous works,
e.g.
Refs.
Giacosa et al. 2005a
;
Divotgey et al. 2013
;
Giacosa et al. 2005b
;
Giacosa et al. 2005c
;
Koenigstein and Giacosa 2016
;
Piotrowska et al. 2017
3
3

3

Note, Ref.
Wang et al. 2017a
included higher derivative couplings and form factors in their approach to strong decays of spin-
3
mesons involving charm quarks.
. Later on, in Chap.
V
, we shall also argue how to justify this strategy using insights from the extended linear sigma model (eLSM) and the Functional Renormalization Group (FRG) approaches to low energy QCD. The explicit form of the Lagrangians (after having carried out the traces) in Table
3
are reported in App.
B
. Each Lagrangian represents the dominant contribution in the large-
N_{\mathrm{c}}
expansion in the given channel, hence each coupling constant scales as
1/\sqrt{N_{\mathrm{c}}}
.

II.2.2
Decay width

The tree level decay widths have the following general form
Zyla et al. 2020
;
Peskin and Schroeder 1995
,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{W_{3}\rightarrow A+B}(m_{w_{3}},m_{a},m_{b})=\vphantom{\frac{\big|\vec{k}_{a,b}\big|}{8\uppi\,m_{w_{3}}^{2}}}

(23)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\frac{\big|\vec{k}_{a,b}\big|}{8\uppi\,m_{w_{3}}^{2}}\,|\mathcal{M}|^{2}\,\kappa_{i}\,\Theta(m_{w_{3}}-m_{a}-m_{b})\,,

where
m_{w_{3}}
is the mass of a (decaying) spin-
3
particle, while
m_{a}
and
m_{b}
are the masses of the decay products “
A
” and “
B
”,
\Theta(x)
denotes the Heaviside step function, and the modulus of the outgoing particles momentum has the following analytic expression,

\displaystyle|\vec{k}_{a,b}|\equiv\frac{1}{2\,m_{w_{3}}}\sqrt{(m_{w_{3}}^{2}-m_{a}^{2}-m_{b}^{2})^{2}-4\,m_{a}^{2}\,m_{b}^{2}}\,.

(24)

We obtain the factors
\kappa_{i}
in Eq. (
23
) for the
i
-th decay channel from the explicit forms of the Lagrangians in the tables in App.
B
by considering the square of the coefficients for a given decay channel as well as eventual sum over members of the same isospin multiplet. The
\kappa_{i}
for all interaction Lagrangians and all relevant channels are presented in App.
C
. The decay amplitudes
|\mathcal{M}|^{2}
are derived via Feynman rules under the use of the polarization vectors and tensors as well as their corresponding completeness relations in App.
D
. The results for
\frac{1}{7}|\mathcal{M}|^{2}
are listed in
4
4

4

Since the decaying particles are spin-
3
mesons we multiply by the factor
\frac{1}{7}
, averaging over the different spin states.
Table
4
.

Decay amplitudes for different decay modes.

decay mode

\frac{1}{7}\,|\mathcal{M}|^{2}

g_{w_{3}pp}^{2}\,\frac{2}{35}\,\big|\vec{k}_{p^{(1)},p^{(2)}}\big|^{6}

3^{--}\rightarrow 0^{-+}+1^{--}

g_{w_{3}v_{1}p}^{2}\,\frac{8}{105}\,\big|\vec{k}_{v_{1},p}\big|^{6}\,m_{w_{3}}^{2}

3^{--}\rightarrow 0^{-+}+2^{++}

g_{w_{3}a_{2}p}^{2}\,\frac{2}{105}\,\big|\vec{k}_{a_{2},p}\big|^{4}\,\frac{m_{w_{3}}^{2}}{m_{a_{2}}^{2}}\,\big(2\,\big|\vec{k}_{a_{2},p}\big|^{2}+7\,m_{a_{2}}^{2}\big)

3^{--}\rightarrow 0^{-+}+1^{+-}

g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}^{2}\,\frac{2}{105}\,\big|\vec{k}_{b_{1},p}\big|^{4}\,\Big(7+3\,\frac{|\vec{k}_{b_{1},p}|^{2}}{m_{b_{1}}^{2}}\Big)

3^{--}\rightarrow 0^{-+}+1^{++}

g_{w_{3}a_{1}p}^{2}\,\frac{2}{105}\,\big|\vec{k}_{a_{1},p}\big|^{4}\,\Big(7+3\,\frac{|\vec{k}_{a_{1},p}|^{2}}{m_{a_{1}}^{2}}\Big)

3^{--}\rightarrow 1^{--}+1^{--}

g_{w_{3}v_{1}v_{1}}^{2}\,\frac{1}{105}\,\big(m_{v_{1}^{(1)}}^{2}\,m_{v_{1}^{(2)}}^{2}\big)^{-1}\,\big|\vec{k}_{v_{1}^{(1)},v_{2}^{(2)}}\big|^{2}\,\big[6\,|\vec{k}_{v_{1}^{(1)},v_{1}^{(2)}}\big|^{4}+35\,m_{v_{1}^{(1)}}^{2}\,m_{v_{1}^{(2)}}^{2}+14\,\big|\vec{k}_{v_{1}^{(1)},v_{1}^{(2)}}\big|^{2}\,\big(m_{v_{1}^{(1)}}^{2}+m_{v_{1}^{(2)}}^{2}\big)\big]

II.2.3
Remarks on the exclusion of scalar mesons

As a final side remark, we note that we cannot couple the spin-
3
nonet (
20
) to scalar mesons (
19
). In fact, if we try to couple them to scalars and pseudoscalars, the only possible CPT- and flavor-invariant interaction term,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\,\mathrm{tr}\big(\partial^{\mu}\mathchoice{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{3}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}{\beta}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{3}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}{\beta}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{3}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}{\beta}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{3}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}{\beta}}}}\,\big[(\partial^{\rho}S),\,(\partial^{\sigma}\partial^{\alpha}\partial^{\beta}P)\big]_{-}\big)=0\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(25)

vanishes identically since it involves the contraction of antisymmetric and symmetric tensors. Correspondingly, scalar mesons do not enter our study, which agrees with the experimental results reported in the PDG
Zyla et al. 2020
. This is also of direct advantage for our work, since we do not have to deal with the identification of the scalar states, which, as already mentioned, is a long-standing and yet unsolved issue of low-energy QCD
Amsler and Close 1995
;
Amsler and Close 1996
;
Strohmeier-Presicek et al. 1999
;
Lee and Weingarten 1999
;
Cheng et al. 2006
;
Gutsche et al. 2016
.

II.2.4
Suppression of next to leading order terms

The Lagrangian terms considered in Table
3
are large-
N_{\mathrm{c}}
(and thus Okubo-Zweig-Iizuka) dominant and symmetric under
U_{\mathrm{V}}(3)
. As we shall discuss later on, this approximation is expected to be acceptable in view of the precision of the experimental data presently available. Yet, by considering the
W_{3}B_{1}P
interaction as an example, the Lagrangian
\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}b_{1}p}
can be regarded as the first term of an expansion in large-
N_{\mathrm{c}}
and/or symmetry breaking terms that takes the form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}b_{1}p}^{\text{full}}=\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(26)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[W^{\mu\nu\rho}\big\{B_{1\mu}\,,(\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\rho}P)\big\}_{+}\big]+\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

\displaystyle+g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}^{(2)}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[W^{\mu\nu\rho}\big]\,\mathrm{tr}\big[B_{1\mu}\,(\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\rho}P)\big]+\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

\displaystyle+g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}^{(3)}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[W^{\mu\nu\rho}\big]\,\mathrm{tr}\big[B_{1\mu}\big]\,\mathrm{tr}\big[\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\rho}P\big]+\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

\displaystyle+g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}^{(4)}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[\hat{\delta}\,W^{\mu\nu\rho}\,\big\{B_{1\mu}\,,(\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\rho}P)\big\}_{+}\big]+\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

\displaystyle+g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}^{(5)}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[\hat{\delta}\,W^{\mu\nu\rho}\big]\,\mathrm{tr}\big[B_{1\mu}\,(\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\rho}P)\big]+\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

\displaystyle+g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}^{(6)}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[W^{\mu\nu\rho}\big]\,\mathrm{tr}\big[\hat{\delta}\,B_{1\mu}\,(\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\rho}P)\big]+\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

\displaystyle+\ldots\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

The first term, reported in Table
3
, is a flavor symmetric term and scales as
g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}\propto N_{\mathrm{c}}^{-1/2},
hence it is the dominant term in a large-
N_{\mathrm{c}}
expansion. The second and the third term are also flavor symmetric, but scale as
g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}^{(2)}\propto N_{\mathrm{\mathrm{c}}}^{-\frac{3}{2}}
and
g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}^{(3)}\propto N_{\mathrm{c}}^{-\frac{5}{2}}
, respectively. They involve gluon exchanges and are therefore suppressed. The fourth term is not large-
N_{\mathrm{c}}
suppressed, but it breaks flavor symmetry via the matrix

\displaystyle\hat{\delta}=\mathrm{diag}\{0,\delta_{d},\delta_{s}\}\,.

(27)

Isospin violation is proportional to
\delta_{d}\propto m_{d}-m_{u}
and is expected to be very small, yet the breaking due to the
s
quark
\delta_{s}\propto m_{s}-m_{u}
can be nonnegligible following Ref.
Amsler and Close 1996
;
Amsler and Close 1995
, it could be along the order of
g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}^{(4)}\simeq g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}\,\mathrm{diag}\{0,0,0.1\,-\,0.2\}
, but the actual value for
J=3
mesons should be determined by an independent fit to data. It is however expected to be sufficiently small to be neglected in this work. At this stage, the first large-
N_{\mathrm{c}}
correction
g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}^{(2)}
and the first flavor symmetric correction
g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}^{(4)}
are expected to be of the same intensity and should be the first to be included. Further terms
g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}^{(n\geq 5)}
are both large-
N_{\mathrm{c}}
subdominant and flavor suppressed, thus are regarded to be very small.

The same analysis can be carried out for all the interaction terms, yet it is interesting to observe that the second and the third term would vanish whenever the commutator is present. (Flavor-symmetry violation is expected to be the main next-to-leading-order contribution for those interaction terms).

III
Phenomenology of the
J^{PC}=3^{--}
nonet

In this section we present our results for decay rates and branching ratios of the
J^{PC}=3^{--}
mesons. In each subsection and for each interaction Lagrangian term, we compare the experimental data to our theoretical results.

We recall that the total decay widths of the
J^{PC}=3^{--}
mesons under consideration are
Zyla et al. 2020
,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\rho_{3}(1690)}^{\text{tot}}=\,

\displaystyle(161\pm 10)\,\,\text{MeV}\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(28)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{K_{3}^{\ast}(1780)}^{\text{tot}}=\,

\displaystyle(159\pm 21)\,\,\text{MeV}\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(29)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\omega_{3}(1670)}^{\text{tot}}=\,

\displaystyle(168\pm 10)\,\,\text{MeV}\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(30)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\phi_{3}(1850)}^{\text{tot}}=\,

\displaystyle(87_{-23}^{+28})\,\,\text{MeV}\,.\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(31)

We shall verify that the sum of all the single decay channels from our theoretical calculations never overshoots these values.

For what concerns the accuracy of our results, whenever possible we determine the coupling and its error via a simple fit of experimental decay width and ratios. We ignore the experimental uncertainties for all masses of the particles of the model and assume the masses to be exact, because their errors are small and they are of minor importance for the overall errors. The experimental errors for the decay widths and branching ratios as well as the systematic errors in the model are much larger, which justifies this approximation. Yet, the quoted errors represents a lower(!) limit of the actual error of this work, since other indeterminacy features are inherent in the approximations, that lead us to the effective action, where only those terms are included, which are expected to be dominant in terms of large-
N_{\mathrm{c}}
, flavor- and momentum-space expansions. Moreover, the decay widths are calculated at tree level. Since the width/mass ratio for the decaying resonances is rather small, contributions due to loops are expected to be negligible
Giacosa and Pagliara 2007
;
Schneitzer et al. 2014
, if seen from a perturbative perspective. Otherwise, if the interaction terms are interpreted as effective couplings in a full quantum effective IR action, calculations have to be performed at tree level anyhow.

III.1
Decay process
W_{3}\rightarrow P+P

The effective interaction term describing the decay of spin-
3
tensor mesons into two pseudoscalar mesons has the following form

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}pp}=g_{w_{3}pp}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[W_{3}^{\mu\nu\rho}\,\big[P,\,(\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\rho}P)\big]_{-}\big]\,.

(32)

Its extended version is listed in App.
B
in Eq. (
126
). Correspondingly, the tree level decay rate is

\displaystyle\Gamma_{W_{3}\rightarrow P^{(1)}+P^{(2)}}(m_{w_{3}},m_{p^{(1)}},m_{p^{(2)}})=\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

(33)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle g_{w_{3}pp}^{2}\,\frac{\big|\vec{k}_{p^{(1)},p^{(2)}}\big|^{7}}{140\uppi\,m_{w_{3}}^{2}}\,\kappa_{i}\,\Theta(m_{w_{3}}-m_{p^{(1)}}-m_{p^{(2)}})\,,\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

compare Eq. (
148
). The factors
\kappa_{i}
are reported in Table
19
. In order to determine the coupling constant
g_{w_{3}pp}
, we use the above formula and the following experimental data:

1.

For
\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow\pi\,\pi
one finds
\kappa_{1}=1
. Using the experimental result

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\mathrm{exp}}_{\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow\pi\,\pi}=(38.0\pm 3.2)\,\,\text{MeV}\,,

(34)

one obtains a first determination of the coupling constant squared and its error. We shall denote them as
\tilde{g}_{1}^{2}
and
\Delta\tilde{g}_{1}^{2}
.

2.

The experimental value

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\text{exp}}_{\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow\bar{K}\,K}=(2.54\pm 0.45)\,\,\text{MeV}\,,

(35)

together with
\kappa_{2}=2\,\big(\frac{1}{2}\big)^{2}
yields an independent determinations of
\tilde{g}_{2}^{2}
and
\Delta\tilde{g}_{2}^{2}
.

3.

From

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\text{exp}}_{K_{3}^{\ast}(1780)\rightarrow\pi\,\bar{K}}=(29.9\pm 4.3)\,\,\text{MeV}

(36)

and
\kappa_{3}=\big(\frac{1}{2}\big)^{2}+\big(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\big)^{2}
we get
\tilde{g}_{3}^{2}
and
\Delta\tilde{g}_{3}^{2}
.

4.

Finally, from

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\text{exp}}_{K_{3}^{\ast}(1780)\rightarrow\bar{K}\,\eta}=(48\pm 22)\,\,\text{MeV}

(37)

and
\kappa_{4}=\big[\frac{1}{2}\,\big(-\cos\beta_{p}+\sqrt{2}\sin\beta_{p}\big)\big]^{2}
we obtain
\tilde{g}_{4}
and
\Delta\,\tilde{g}_{4}
.

The coupling constant and its indeterminacy are evaluated using a simple
\chi^{2}
-approach,
5
5

5

We are aware of the fact that some of the data might be correlated, because it is taken from similar measurements at the same experiments. Within the level of accuracy of our approach, there is no need to take care of these effects.

\displaystyle g_{w_{3}pp}^{2}=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{\tilde{g}_{i}}{\Delta\tilde{g}_{i}^{2}}}{\sum_{j=1}^{4}\frac{1}{\Delta\tilde{g}_{j}^{2}}}\,,

\displaystyle\Delta g_{w_{3}pp}^{2}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{\sum_{j=1}^{4}\frac{1}{\Delta g_{j}^{2}}}}\,,

(38)

which results in

g_{w_{3}pp}^{2}=(1.5\pm 0.1)\cdot 10^{-10}\,\,\text{MeV}^{-4}\,.

(39)

The comparison of theoretical and experimental results, which is obtained by using this value for the coupling constant, is reported in Table
5
. A good overall agreement is obtained, but there is also a sizable mismatch: the experimental value for
K_{3}^{\ast}(1780)\rightarrow\bar{K}\,\eta
is much larger than our theoretical prediction. Still, the experimental error is large and a better experimental determination would be interesting. Moreover, a noteworthy prediction concerning
\phi_{3}(1850)\rightarrow\bar{K}\,K
is obtained. From the theoretical large prediction, we conclude that an experimental determination should be feasible.

.

III.2
Decay process
W_{3}\rightarrow V_{1}+P

The interaction Lagrangian for the vector and pseudoscalar decay mode reads

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}v_{1}p}=\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(40)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle g_{w_{3}v_{1}p}\,\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[W_{3,\mu\alpha\beta}\,\big\{(\partial_{\nu}V_{1,\rho}),\,(\partial^{\alpha}\partial^{\beta}\partial_{\sigma}P)\big\}_{+}\big]\,.\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

An extended version is given by Eq. (
127
) in App.
B
. In this case, the tree level decay rate formula has the form

\displaystyle\Gamma_{W_{3}\rightarrow V_{1}+P}(m_{w_{3}},m_{v_{1}},m_{p})=\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

(41)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle g_{w_{3}v_{1}p}^{2}\,\frac{\big|\vec{k}_{v_{1},p}\big|^{7}}{105\uppi}\,\kappa_{i}\,\Theta(m_{w_{3}}-m_{v_{1}}-m_{p})\,,\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

see also Eq. (
151
), where the factors
\kappa_{i}
are reported in Table
30
. In order to define the coupling constant we proceed as in the pseudoscalar-pseudoscalar case. We use

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\text{exp}}_{\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow\omega(782)\,\pi}=\,

\displaystyle(25.8\pm 9.8)\,\,\text{MeV}\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\text{exp}}_{K_{3}^{\ast}(1780)\rightarrow\rho(770)\,K}=\,

\displaystyle(49.3\pm 15.7)\,\,\text{MeV}\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\text{exp}}_{K_{3}^{\ast}(1780)\rightarrow\bar{K}^{\ast}(892)\,\pi}=\,

\displaystyle(31.8\pm 9.0)\,\,\text{MeV}\,.\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

The coupling constant and its error are

\displaystyle g_{w_{3}v_{1}p}^{2}=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{3}\frac{\tilde{g}_{i}}{\Delta\tilde{g}_{i}^{2}}}{\sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{1}{\Delta\tilde{g}_{j}^{2}}}\,,

\displaystyle\Delta g_{w_{3}v_{1}p}^{2}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{\sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{1}{\Delta g_{j}^{2}}}}\,,

(42)

hence

\displaystyle g_{w_{3}v_{1}p}^{2}=(9.2\pm 1.9)\cdot 10^{-16}\,\,\text{MeV}^{-6}

(43)

This value leads to the results listed in Table
6
. We observe that an acceptable agreement is reached, although the
\Gamma_{K_{3}^{\ast}(1780)\rightarrow\rho(770)\,K}
mode is theoretically underestimated (the experimental error is nevertheless large). Quite remarkably, the two theoretically sizable and dominant decays
\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow\rho(770)\,K
and
\phi_{3}(1850)\rightarrow K^{\ast}(892)\,K
have been indeed seen in experiments, though they could not be quantified. Their future determination would represent a test of our approach – at least on a qualitative level.

.

In addition, we present the following ratio

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\phi_{3}(1850)\rightarrow\bar{K}^{\ast}(892)\,K}}{\Gamma_{\phi_{3}(1850)\rightarrow\bar{K}\,K}}=1.9\pm 0.4\,,

(44)

which does not totally contradict the PDG
Zyla et al. 2020
average taken from Ref.
Aston et al. 1988
,

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma^{\text{exp}}_{\phi_{3}(1850)\rightarrow\bar{K}^{\ast}(892)\,K}}{\Gamma^{\text{exp}}_{\phi_{3}(1850)\rightarrow\bar{K}\,K}}=0.55_{-0.45}^{+0.85}\,.

(45)

Interestingly Ref.
Al-Harran et al. 1981
even reports a slightly larger ratio of

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma^{\text{exp}}_{\phi_{3}(1850)\rightarrow\bar{K}^{\ast}(892)\,K}}{\Gamma^{\text{exp}}_{\phi_{3}(1850)\rightarrow\bar{K}\,K}}=0.8\pm 0.4\,.

(46)

Furthermore, we would like to mention a recent lattice QCD study
Johnson and Dudek 2020
, which also confirms our overall predictions on dominant and less dominant vector-pseudoscalar decay channels. Reference
Johnson and Dudek 2020
predicts (without providing explicit errors due to large uncertainties),

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\text{J,D}}_{\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow\omega(782)\,\pi}=\,

\displaystyle 22\,\,\text{MeV}\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(47)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\text{J,D}}_{\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow\bar{K}^{\ast}(892)\,K}=\,

\displaystyle 2\,\,\text{MeV}\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(48)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\text{J,D}}_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow\rho(770)\,\pi}=\,

\displaystyle 62\,\,\text{MeV}\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(49)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\text{J,D}}_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow\bar{K}^{\ast}(892)\,K}=\,

\displaystyle 2\,\,\text{MeV}\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(50)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\text{J,D}}_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow\omega(782)\,\eta}=\,

\displaystyle 1\,\,\text{MeV}\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(51)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\text{J,D}}_{\phi_{3}(1850)\rightarrow\bar{K}^{\ast}(892)\,K}=\,

\displaystyle 20\,\,\text{MeV}\,,\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(52)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\text{J,D}}_{\phi_{3}(1850)\rightarrow\phi(1020)\,\eta}=\,

\displaystyle 3\,\,\text{MeV}\,.\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(53)

It is quite remarkable that our (quite simple) model is able to predict at least qualitatively rather similar results to such an advanced and comprehensive lattice QCD study. This leads us to the conclusion, that even for high-spin mesons with masses above
1\,\,
GeV like the conventional
3^{--}
-nonet chiral symmetry (breaking) is the decisive guiding principle for their phenomenology.

III.3
Decay process
W_{3}\rightarrow\gamma+P

As a next step, we also present the results for the radioactive decays
W_{3}\rightarrow\gamma\,P
, where
\gamma
represents the photon. These can be obtained by using vector meson dominance
Ebert and Volkov 1983
;
O’Connell et al. 1997
;
Kim et al. 1996
, which takes into account of the photon-vector-meson mixing through the shift

\displaystyle V_{\mu\nu}\mapsto V_{\mu\nu}+\tfrac{eQ}{g_{\rho}}\,F_{\mu\nu}\,,

(54)

where
V_{\mu\nu}\equiv\partial_{\mu}V_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}V_{\mu}
and
Q=\mathrm{diag}\big(\frac{2}{3},-\frac{1}{3},\,-\frac{1}{3}\big)
is the charge quark matrix, which includes the charges of the up, down, and strange quark. The electromagnetic field tensor is denoted as
F_{\mu\nu}=\partial_{\mu}a_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}a_{\mu}
, with
a_{\mu}
being the photon field, while
e=\sqrt{4\uppi\,\alpha}
is the electric coupling constant, and
g_{\rho}\simeq 5.5
parametrizes the photon-vector-meson transition. By applying the shift of Eq. (
54
) into Eq. (
40
) we obtain the Lagrangian for radioactive decays

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}\gamma p}=\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(55)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle g_{w_{3}v_{1}p}\,\tfrac{e}{g_{\rho}}\,\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\,(\partial_{\nu}a_{\rho})\,\mathrm{tr}\big[W_{3,\mu\alpha\beta}\,\big\{Q,\,(\partial^{\alpha}\partial^{\beta}\partial_{\sigma}P)\big\}_{+}\big]\,.\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

The extended version of this Lagrangian is provided by Eq. (
128
) in App.
B
. For the tree level decay rate formula, we obtain via Eq. (
154
),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{W_{3}\rightarrow\gamma+P}(m_{w_{3}},m_{p})=\,

\displaystyle g_{w_{3}v_{1}p}^{2}\,\big(\tfrac{e}{g_{\rho}}\big)^{2}\,\frac{\big|\vec{k}_{\gamma,p}\big|^{7}}{105\pi}\,\kappa_{i}^{\gamma}\,.\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

(56)

where one of the masses
m_{a}
and
m_{b}
in Eq. (
24
) is set to zero, because the photon is massless. The factors
\kappa_{i}^{\gamma}
are listed in Table
20
. (The Heaviside function is not needed, because all pseudoscalars are lighter than the conventional spin-
3
mesons and the photons can be arbitrarily soft.)

Various predictions for the radiative decays
W_{3}\rightarrow\gamma\,P
are calculated and presented in Table
7
. Because of the rather large errors in the coupling constant (
43
), we round all results to integers in units of keV. Quite large radiative decay channels are
\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow\gamma\,\pi^{0}
as well as
K_{3}^{0}(1780)\rightarrow\gamma\,K^{0}
.

In general, these processes imply that a photoproduction of mesons with spin
J^{PC}=3^{--}
can take place at the ongoing
GlueX

Al Ghoul et al. 2016
;
Zihlmann 2010
;
Shepherd 2014
and
CLAS12

Rizzo 2016
experiments at
Jefferson Lab
.

III.4
Decay process
W_{3}\rightarrow A_{2}+P

The interaction Lagrangian describing the decay of the spin-
3
tensor mesons into a spin-
2
tensor and a pseudoscalar mesons has the following form

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}a_{2}p}=\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(57)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle g_{w_{3}a_{2}p}\,\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[\mathchoice{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{3}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}{\beta}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{3}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}{\beta}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{3}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}{\beta}}}}{W^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{3}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha}{\beta}}}}\,\big[(\partial^{\nu}A_{2}^{\rho\alpha}),\,(\partial^{\sigma}\partial^{\beta}P)\big]_{-}\big]\,.\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

The extended version is provided in App.
B
in Eq. (
129
). Using Eq. (
157
) the corresponding decay formula is

\displaystyle\Gamma_{W_{3}\rightarrow A_{2}+P}(m_{w_{3}},m_{a_{2}},m_{p})=\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

(58)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle g_{w_{3}a_{2}p}^{2}\,\frac{\big|\vec{k}_{a_{2},p}\big|^{5}}{420\uppi\,m_{a_{2}}^{2}}\,\big(2\,\big|\vec{k}_{a_{2},p}\big|^{2}+7\,m_{a_{2}}^{2}\big)\,\kappa_{i}\times\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle\times\Theta(m_{w_{3}}-m_{a_{2}}-m_{p})\,,\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

where the
\kappa_{i}
are listed in Table
21
. For this channel, there are no experimental reported branching ratios that allow for a direct determination of the coupling constant. Yet, we can use the experimental ratio listed by the PDG
Zyla et al. 2020
and taken from Ref.
Amelin et al. 2000
,

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma^{\text{exp}}_{\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow a_{2}(1320)\,\pi}}{\Gamma^{\text{exp}}_{\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow\rho(770)\,\eta}}=5.5\pm 2.0\,.

(59)

together with the previously determined theoretical value
\Gamma_{\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow\rho(770)\,\eta}=(3.8\pm 0.8)\,\,
MeV reported in Table
6
. The corresponding value for the coupling constant is

\displaystyle g_{w_{3}a_{2}p}^{2}=(2.8\pm 1.2)\cdot 10^{-9}\,\,\text{MeV}^{-4}\,.

(60)

Once the coupling constant is fixed, we get the results for the decay rates reported in Table
8
. The decay width
\Gamma_{K_{3}^{\ast}(1780)\rightarrow\bar{K}^{\ast}_{2}(1430)\,\pi}
is safely smaller the experimental upper limit
Zyla et al. 2020
;
Aston et al. 1987
. Moreover, the quite large mode
\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow a_{2}(1320)\,\pi
is seen in experiments, but also in this case no branching ratio is listed in the PDG
Zyla et al. 2020
.

III.5
Decay process
W_{3}\rightarrow B_{1}+P

The interaction Lagrangian describing the decay into a pseudovector and pseudoscalar meson is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}b_{1}p}=g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[W_{3}^{\mu\nu\rho}\,\big\{B_{1,\mu},\,(\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\rho}P)\big\}_{+}\big]\,,

(61)

where the extended form is given in Eq. (
130
) in App.
B
. The tree level decay rate takes the form,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{W_{3}\rightarrow B_{1}+P}(m_{w_{3}},m_{b_{1}},m_{p})=\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

(62)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}^{2}\,\frac{|\vec{k}_{b_{1},p}|^{5}}{420\uppi\,m_{w_{3}}^{2}}\,\Bigg(7+3\,\frac{\big|\vec{k}_{b_{1},p}\big|^{2}}{m_{b_{1}}^{2}}\Bigg)\,\kappa_{i}\times\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle\times\Theta(m_{w_{3}}-m_{b_{1}}-m_{p})\,.\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

Here, we use Eq. (
162
) and the
\kappa_{i}
can be found in Table
22
. Also in this case, a direct determination of the coupling constant
g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}
is not possible due to the lack of experimental information. In order to estimate it, we proceed as it follows.

1.

According to the PDG
Zyla et al. 2020
, there exists a lower limit for the ratio

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow b_{1}(1235)\,\pi}}{\Gamma_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow\omega(782)\,\pi\,\pi}}>0.75\,.

(63)

The PDG
Zyla et al. 2020
extracts this lower bound for the ratio from Ref.
Baubillier et al. 1979
and does not list this bound as confirmed data. In Ref.
Baubillier et al. 1979
the authors in fact report

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow b_{1}(1235)\,\pi}}{\Gamma_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow\omega(782)\,\pi\,\pi}}=1.0_{-0.25}^{+0.0}\,.

(64)

They further argue that the decay mode
\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow b_{1}(1235)\,\pi
is the dominant contribution to the
\omega(782)\,\pi\,\pi
final state via the subsequent decay
b_{1}(1235)\,\rightarrow\omega(782)\,\pi
. They claim that one can assume that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow b_{1}(1235)\,\pi}\approx\Gamma_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow\omega(782)\,\pi\,\pi}

(65)

and deviations are expected to be smaller than
10\%
.

2.

Based on this assumption we use the following approximation

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow b_{1}(1235)\,\pi}}{\Gamma_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow\rho(770)\,\pi}}\overset{\text{Eq.\ \eqref{saturation}}}{\approx}

(66)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Eq.\ \eqref{saturation}}}{\approx}\,

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow\omega\,\pi\,\pi}}{\Gamma_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow\rho(770)\,\pi}}\overset{\text{PDG}}{=}0.71\pm 0.27\,.

Here, one should mention that the PDG
Zyla et al. 2020
cites Ref.
Diaz et al. 1974
for the second ratio, but still excludes it from their confirmed data. Additionally, Ref.
Diaz et al. 1974
even lists a different branching ratio,

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow\omega\,\pi\,\pi}}{\Gamma_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow\rho(770)\,\pi}}=0.47\pm 0.18\,,

(67)

as well as

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow b_{1}(1235)\,\pi}}{\Gamma_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow\rho(770)\,\pi}}=0.32\pm 0.16\,,

(68)

It is not clear to the authors of this work, how the PDG
Zyla et al. 2020
extracted their ratio from Ref.
Diaz et al. 1974
. Nevertheless, we will stick to the data reported in the PDG
Zyla et al. 2020
and assume that there was some reasonable reanalysis of the data of Ref.
Diaz et al. 1974
. In this context, it might be worth mentioning, that all experimental data concerning the
\omega_{3}(1670)
is rather old and its mass and total width could not be determined up to high accuracy at the time when Refs.
Baubillier et al. 1979
;
Diaz et al. 1974
were published. Definitely, there is a need for future experimental investigations of this state.

For what concerns our work, we use the result
\Gamma_{\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow\rho(770)\,\pi}=(97\pm 20)\,\,
MeV presented in the previous section as well as the ratio (
66
). We obtain the following estimate for the coupling constant

\displaystyle g_{w_{3}b_{1}p}^{2}\approx(0.008\pm 0.003)\,\,\text{MeV}^{-2}\,.

(69)

We are aware that this value and the corresponding results, which are listed in Table
9
, are only first rough estimates. Still, they might again help identifying dominant decay channels. Additionally, we consider the following aspects for the determination and interpretation of our results.

\beta_{b_{1}}=0
and
\beta_{b_{1}}=-40^{\circ}
, respectively. No experimental value is listed in the PDG
Zyla et al. 2020
. The decay channel
\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow b_{1}(1235)\,\pi
is possibly seen.

69\pm 30

\phi_{3}(1850)\rightarrow\bar{K}_{1,B}\,K+\mathrm{c}.\mathrm{c}.

9.1\pm 3.9

\phi_{3}(1850)\rightarrow h_{1}(1170)\,\eta

0.006\pm 0.003

1.1\pm 0.5

1.

We consider
K_{1,B}\approx K_{1}(1270)
, since this is the dominant contribution; then, the decay rate
\Gamma_{\phi_{3}(1850)\rightarrow\bar{K}_{1,B}\,K}
is rather small. A disclaimer is in order: due to the large mixing between
K_{1,A}
and
K_{1,B}
the results involving the identification
K_{1,B}\approx K_{1}(1270)
can be only considered as a first approximation. The more correct procedure should be to consider the full mixing in Eq. (
17
), hence
K_{1,B}
should be expressed as a superposition of
K_{1}(1270)
and
K_{1}(1400)
. Yet, this is not an easy task, since the interaction in Eq. (
61
) alone is not enough. One should also take into account the interaction terms for the decays
W_{3}\rightarrow A_{1}\,P
in Eq. (
70
), which includes
K_{1,A}
, see next section. Then, the decays into
K_{1}(1270)
and
K_{1}(1400)
should be calculated by the joint Lagrangians (
61
) and (
70
) together with the mixing (
17
). Furthermore, interference between the two Lagrangians is expected. This calculation is however not possible, since the coupling constant
g_{w_{3}a_{1}p}
cannot be determined by the present experimental data (see next subsection). Hence, we must limit our study to the dominant assignment
K_{1,B}\approx K_{1}(1270)
(in this subsection) and
K_{1,A}\approx K_{1}(1400)
(in the next subsection).

2.

We present the results in Table
9
for two values of the unknown mixing angle in the isoscalar sector. In one case, we assume that the mixing angle vanishes,
\beta_{b_{1}}\approx 0
, in the second we study
\beta_{b_{1}}\approx-40^{\circ}
, which is a quite large and negative value similar to the mixing angle for the pseudoscalar-isoscalars. Note, the mixing angle
\beta_{b_{1}}
appears in the decay channels
\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow h_{1}(1170)\,\pi
,
\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow h_{1}(1415)\,\pi
,
K_{3}^{\ast}(1780)\rightarrow h_{1}(1170)\,\bar{K}
, and
\phi_{3}(1850)\rightarrow h_{1}(1170)\,\eta
, see Table
22
. The only experimentally possibly seen decay
\omega_{3}(1670)\rightarrow b_{1}(1235)\,\pi
, see Ref.
Diaz et al. 1974
, corresponds to a quite large theoretical partial decay widths and is independent of any assumption on
\beta_{b_{1}}
.

3.

The decay
\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow h_{1}(1170)\,\pi
is also quite large for both choices of the mixing angle, hence it is a potentially interesting channel for future search. Moreover, it is also an interesting decay channel in order to determine the value of the mixing angle
\beta_{b_{1}}
. The remaining decay channels are pretty small, which might be an explanation why they could not be observed in experiment.

III.6
Decay process
W_{3}\rightarrow A_{1}+P

The interaction Lagrangian and the decay formula for the decay into an axial-vector and a pseudoscalar meson are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}a_{1}p}=g_{w_{3}a_{1}p}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[W_{3}^{\mu\nu\rho}\,\big[A_{1,\mu},\,(\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\rho}P)\big]_{-}\big]\,,

(70)

and

\displaystyle\Gamma_{W_{3}\rightarrow A_{1}+P}(m_{w_{3}},m_{a_{1}},m_{p})=\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

(71)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle g_{w_{3}a_{1}p}^{2}\,\frac{\big|\vec{k}_{a_{1},p}\big|^{5}}{420\uppi\,m_{w_{3}}^{2}}\,\Bigg(7+3\,\frac{\big|\vec{k}_{a_{1},p}\big|^{2}}{m_{a_{1}}^{2}}\Bigg)\,\kappa_{i}\times\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle\times\Theta(m_{w_{3}}-m_{a_{1}}-m_{p})\,.\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

The extended version of the Lagrangian is given by Eq. (
131
) in App.
B
. The
\kappa_{i}
-values for each channel are taken from Table
23
and the decay formula was derived by using Eq. (
163
). As explained previously, for a first rough estimate, we assume here that
K_{1,A}\approx K_{1}(1400)
.

Since we do not have enough experimental information for obtaining the coupling constant
g_{w_{3}a_{1}p}
, we can only get some theoretical predictions for ratios among different decay channels, that are reported in Table
10
. Note, the mixing angle
\beta_{a_{1}}
does not appear in any of the nonzero reported decay channel, as it can be read from Table
23
. Anyway, the mixing is expected to be small
Giacosa et al. 2018
.

Table 10:
Predictions for the nonzero branching ratios of
W_{3}\rightarrow A_{1}\,P
. We choose as a reference the
a_{1}(1260)\,\pi
decay mode.

branching ratio

theory

\frac{\Gamma_{K_{3}^{\ast}(1780)\rightarrow K_{1,A}\,\pi}}{\Gamma_{\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow a_{1}(1260)\,\pi}}

0.12

\frac{\Gamma_{K_{3}^{\ast}(1780)\rightarrow a_{1}(1260)\,K}}{\Gamma_{\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow a_{1}(1260)\,\pi}}

0.01

In the end, it is interesting to mention that there might be an option to size the coupling constant by linking the present model to an underlying chiral model,
e.g.
the eLSM. This approximately predicts
2\,\,
MeV for
\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow a_{1}(1260)\,\pi
, see Chap.
V
for an estimate of
g_{w_{3}a_{1}p}
. It seems therefore that the decays of the type
W_{3}\rightarrow A_{1}+P
are suppressed.

III.7
Decay process
W_{3}\rightarrow V_{1}+V_{1}

The interaction Lagrangian describing the decay into two vector mesons is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{w_{3}v_{1}v_{1}}=g_{w_{3}v_{1}v_{1}}\,\mathrm{tr}\big[W_{3}^{\mu\nu\rho}\,\big[(\partial_{\mu}V_{1,\nu}),\,V_{1,\rho}\big]_{-}\big]\,,

(72)

see Eq. (
132
) in App.
B
for the extended version . The corresponding decay rate reads

\displaystyle\Gamma_{W_{3}\rightarrow V_{1}^{(1)}+V_{1}^{(2)}}(m_{w_{3}},m_{v_{1}^{(1)}},m_{v_{1}^{(2)}})=\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

(73)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle g_{w_{3}v_{1}v_{1}}^{2}\,\frac{\big|\vec{k}_{v_{1}^{(1)},v_{1}^{(2)}}\big|^{3}}{840\uppi\,m_{v_{1}^{(1)}}^{2}\,m_{v_{1}^{(2)}}^{2}\,m_{w_{3}}^{2}}\,\Big[6\,|\vec{k}_{v_{1}^{(1)},v_{1}^{(2)}}\big|^{4}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+35\,m_{v_{1}^{(1)}}^{2}\,m_{v_{1}^{(2)}}^{2}+14\,\big|\vec{k}_{v_{1}^{(1)},v_{1}^{(2)}}\big|^{2}\,\big(m_{v_{1}^{(1)}}^{2}+m_{v_{1}^{(2)}}^{2}\big)\Big]\times\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle\times\kappa_{i}\,\Theta(m_{w_{3}}-m_{v_{1}^{(1)}}-m_{v_{1}^{(2)}})\,,\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

where the
\kappa_{i}
’s are listed in Table
24
. For the derivation of the decay formula, we use Eq. (
166
). Also in this case, we cannot determine the coupling constant, but we can present the branching ratios reported in Table
11
.

Table 11:
Predictions for the branching ratios of
W_{3}\rightarrow V_{1}+V_{1}
. We choose as a reference the
\rho\,\rho
decay mode.

branching ratio

theory

\frac{\Gamma_{K_{3}^{\ast}(1780)\rightarrow\rho(770)\,K^{\ast}(892)}}{\Gamma_{\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow\rho(770)\,\rho(770)}}

Moreover, we can find an upper limit for the coupling constant by considering

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow\pi\,\pi}}{\Gamma_{\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow\rho(770)\,\rho(770)}}\gtrapprox

(74)

\displaystyle\gtrapprox\,

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma_{\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow\pi\,\pi}}{\Gamma_{\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow\pi^{\pm}\,\pi^{+}\,\pi^{-}\,\pi^{0}}}\overset{\text{PDG}}{=}0.35\pm 0.11\,,

which follows from the fact that the
\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow\rho(770)\,\rho(770)
mode is part of the
\pi^{\pm}\,\pi^{+}\,\pi^{-}\,\pi^{0}
decay mode (and eventually it is one of its dominating contributors). Since the experimental value for
\Gamma_{\rho_{3}(1690)\rightarrow\pi\,\pi}=(38.0\pm 3.2)\,\,
MeV is known, we find

\displaystyle g_{w_{3}v_{1}v_{1}}^{2}\lesssim 535\pm 174\,.

(75)

Based on this information we can predict upper limits for the decay rates in Table
12
.
