(194)

\displaystyle 0=\,

\displaystyle k_{\mu}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho}(\lambda,\vec{k})\,.\vphantom{\bigg(\bigg)}

(195)

Next we construct the completeness relation for these polarization tensors. We use the definition

\displaystyle G_{\mu\nu}\equiv\eta_{\mu\nu}-\frac{k_{\mu}k_{\nu}}{k^{2}}

(196)

and write down the most general ansatz for the completeness relation, which contains all possible combinations of the metric and the four momentum or the projection operators (
192
) respectively,

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho}(\lambda,\vec{k})\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\lambda,\vec{k})=\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

(197)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle+a_{1}\,G_{\mu\nu}G_{\rho\alpha}G_{\beta\gamma}+a_{2}\,G_{\mu\nu}G_{\rho\beta}G_{\alpha\gamma}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+a_{3}\,G_{\mu\nu}G_{\rho\gamma}G_{\alpha\beta}+a_{4}\,G_{\mu\rho}G_{\nu\alpha}G_{\beta\gamma}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+a_{5}\,G_{\mu\rho}G_{\nu\beta}G_{\alpha\gamma}+a_{6}\,G_{\mu\rho}G_{\nu\gamma}G_{\alpha\beta}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+a_{7}\,G_{\mu\alpha}G_{\nu\rho}G_{\beta\gamma}+a_{8}\,G_{\mu\alpha}G_{\nu\beta}G_{\rho\gamma}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+a_{9}\,G_{\mu\alpha}G_{\nu\gamma}G_{\rho\beta}+a_{10}\,G_{\mu\beta}G_{\nu\rho}G_{\alpha\gamma}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+a_{11}\,G_{\mu\beta}G_{\nu\alpha}G_{\rho\gamma}+a_{12}\,G_{\mu\beta}G_{\nu\gamma}G_{\rho\alpha}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+a_{13}\,G_{\mu\gamma}G_{\nu\rho}G_{\alpha\beta}+a_{14}\,G_{\mu\gamma}G_{\nu\alpha}G_{\rho\beta}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+a_{15}\,G_{\mu\gamma}G_{\nu\beta}G_{\rho\alpha}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+c_{13}\,G_{\alpha\beta}k_{\mu}k_{\nu}k_{\rho}k_{\gamma}+c_{14}\,G_{\alpha\gamma}k_{\mu}k_{\nu}k_{\rho}k_{\beta}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+c_{15}\,G_{\beta\gamma}k_{\mu}k_{\nu}k_{\rho}k_{\alpha}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+d\,k_{\mu}k_{\nu}k_{\rho}k_{\alpha}k_{\beta}k_{\gamma}\,.\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

Next, we have to determine the coefficients

,

,

and

), (

First, we use Eq. (

yields

\displaystyle a_{4}=a_{7}\,,

\displaystyle a_{5}=a_{10}\,,

\displaystyle a_{6}=a_{13}\,,\vphantom{\frac{1}{2}}

(198)

\displaystyle a_{8}=a_{11}\,,

\displaystyle a_{9}=a_{14}\,,

\displaystyle a_{1}=a_{4}\,,

The last symmetry, which includes all other possible index interchanges, is
\mu\,\nu\,\rho\leftrightarrow\alpha\,\beta\,\gamma
, from which follows that

\displaystyle a_{1}=a_{13}\,,

\displaystyle a_{2}=a_{6}\,,

\displaystyle a_{4}=a_{10}\,,\vphantom{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle a_{12}=a_{14}\,,

\displaystyle b_{19}=b_{34}\,,

\displaystyle b_{29}=b_{42}\,,

\displaystyle c_{9}=c_{11}\,.

Therefore, we define new coefficients

\displaystyle a\equiv\,

\displaystyle a_{1}=a_{2}=a_{3}=a_{4}=a_{5}=a_{6}=a_{7}=a_{10}=a_{13}\,,\vphantom{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle a^{\prime}\equiv\,

\displaystyle a_{8}=a_{9}=a_{11}=a_{12}=a_{14}=a_{15}\,,\vphantom{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle b\equiv\,

\displaystyle b_{1}=b_{2}=b_{3}=b_{7}=b_{8}=b_{9}=b_{13}=b_{18}=b_{19}=\vphantom{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle b_{24}=b_{25}=b_{30}=b_{31}=b_{32}=b_{33}=b_{34}=b_{35}=\vphantom{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle b_{36}\,,\vphantom{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle b^{\prime}\equiv\,

\displaystyle b_{14}=b_{15}=b_{16}=b_{17}=b_{20}=b_{21}=b_{22}=b_{23}=\vphantom{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\tilde{b}\equiv\,

\displaystyle b_{4}=b_{5}=b_{6}=b_{10}=b_{11}=b_{12}=b_{37}=b_{40}=b_{43}\,,\vphantom{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle c\equiv\,

\displaystyle c_{1}=c_{2}=c_{6}=c_{13}=c_{14}=c_{15}\,,\vphantom{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle c^{\prime}\equiv\,

\displaystyle c_{3}=c_{4}=c_{5}=c_{7}=c_{8}=c_{9}=c_{10}=c_{11}=c_{12}\,,\vphantom{\frac{1}{2}}

and replace the coefficients of our ansatz.

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho}(\lambda,\vec{k})\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\lambda,\vec{k})=

(201)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+a\,G_{\mu\nu}G_{\rho\alpha}G_{\beta\gamma}+a\,G_{\mu\nu}G_{\rho\beta}G_{\alpha\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+a\,G_{\mu\nu}G_{\rho\gamma}G_{\alpha\beta}+a\,G_{\mu\rho}G_{\nu\alpha}G_{\beta\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+a\,G_{\mu\rho}G_{\nu\beta}G_{\alpha\gamma}+a\,G_{\mu\rho}G_{\nu\gamma}G_{\alpha\beta}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+a\,G_{\mu\alpha}G_{\nu\rho}G_{\beta\gamma}+a^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\alpha}G_{\nu\beta}G_{\rho\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+a^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\alpha}G_{\nu\gamma}G_{\rho\beta}+a\,G_{\mu\beta}G_{\nu\rho}G_{\alpha\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+a^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\beta}G_{\nu\alpha}G_{\rho\gamma}+a^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\beta}G_{\nu\gamma}G_{\rho\alpha}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+a\,G_{\mu\gamma}G_{\nu\rho}G_{\alpha\beta}+a^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\gamma}G_{\nu\alpha}G_{\rho\beta}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+a^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\gamma}G_{\nu\beta}G_{\rho\alpha}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b\,G_{\mu\nu}G_{\rho\alpha}k_{\beta}k_{\gamma}+b\,G_{\mu\nu}G_{\rho\beta}k_{\alpha}k_{\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b\,G_{\mu\nu}G_{\rho\gamma}k_{\alpha}k_{\beta}+\tilde{b}\,G_{\mu\nu}G_{\alpha\beta}k_{\rho}k_{\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+\tilde{b}\,G_{\mu\nu}G_{\alpha\gamma}k_{\rho}k_{\beta}+\tilde{b}\,G_{\mu\nu}G_{\beta\gamma}k_{\rho}k_{\alpha}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b\,G_{\mu\rho}G_{\nu\alpha}k_{\beta}k_{\gamma}+b\,G_{\mu\rho}G_{\nu\beta}k_{\alpha}k_{\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b\,G_{\mu\rho}G_{\nu\gamma}k_{\alpha}k_{\beta}+\tilde{b}\,G_{\mu\rho}G_{\alpha\beta}k_{\nu}k_{\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+\tilde{b}\,G_{\mu\rho}G_{\alpha\gamma}k_{\nu}k_{\beta}+\tilde{b}\,G_{\mu\rho}G_{\beta\gamma}k_{\nu}k_{\alpha}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b\,G_{\mu\alpha}G_{\nu\rho}k_{\beta}k_{\gamma}+b^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\alpha}G_{\nu\beta}k_{\rho}k_{\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\alpha}G_{\nu\gamma}k_{\rho}k_{\beta}+b^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\alpha}G_{\rho\beta}k_{\nu}k_{\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b^{\prime}G_{\mu\alpha}G_{\rho\gamma}k_{\nu}k_{\beta}+b\,G_{\mu\alpha}G_{\beta\gamma}k_{\nu}k_{\rho}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b\,G_{\mu\beta}G_{\nu\rho}k_{\alpha}k_{\gamma}+b^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\beta}G_{\nu\alpha}k_{\rho}k_{\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\beta}G_{\nu\gamma}k_{\rho}k_{\alpha}+b^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\beta}G_{\rho\alpha}k_{\nu}k_{\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b^{\prime}G_{\mu\beta}G_{\rho\gamma}k_{\nu}k_{\alpha}+b\,G_{\mu\beta}G_{\alpha\gamma}k_{\nu}k_{\rho}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b\,G_{\mu\gamma}G_{\nu\rho}k_{\alpha}k_{\beta}+b^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\gamma}G_{\nu\alpha}k_{\rho}k_{\beta}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\gamma}G_{\nu\beta}k_{\rho}k_{\alpha}+b^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\gamma}G_{\rho\alpha}k_{\nu}k_{\beta}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b^{\prime}G_{\mu\gamma}G_{\rho\beta}k_{\nu}k_{\alpha}+b\,G_{\mu\gamma}G_{\alpha\beta}k_{\nu}k_{\rho}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b\,k_{\mu}k_{\nu}G_{\rho\alpha}G_{\beta\gamma}+b\,k_{\mu}k_{\nu}G_{\rho\beta}G_{\alpha\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b\,k_{\mu}k_{\nu}G_{\rho\gamma}G_{\alpha\beta}+b\,k_{\mu}k_{\rho}G_{\nu\alpha}G_{\beta\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b\,k_{\mu}k_{\rho}G_{\nu\beta}G_{\alpha\gamma}+b\,k_{\mu}k_{\rho}G_{\nu\gamma}G_{\alpha\beta}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+\tilde{b}\,k_{\mu}k_{\alpha}G_{\nu\rho}G_{\beta\gamma}+b^{\prime}k_{\mu}k_{\alpha}G_{\nu\beta}G_{\rho\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b^{\prime}\,k_{\mu}k_{\alpha}G_{\nu\gamma}G_{\rho\beta}+\tilde{b}\,k_{\mu}k_{\beta}G_{\nu\rho}G_{\alpha\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b^{\prime}k_{\mu}k_{\beta}G_{\nu\alpha}G_{\rho\gamma}+b^{\prime}\,k_{\mu}k_{\beta}G_{\nu\gamma}G_{\rho\alpha}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+\tilde{b}\,k_{\mu}k_{\gamma}G_{\nu\rho}G_{\alpha\beta}+b^{\prime}k_{\mu}k_{\gamma}G_{\nu\alpha}G_{\rho\beta}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+b^{\prime}\,k_{\mu}k_{\gamma}G_{\nu\beta}G_{\rho\alpha}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+c\,G_{\mu\nu}k_{\rho}k_{\alpha}k_{\beta}k_{\gamma}+c\,G_{\mu\rho}k_{\nu}k_{\alpha}k_{\beta}k_{\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+c^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\alpha}k_{\nu}k_{\rho}k_{\beta}k_{\gamma}+c^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\beta}k_{\nu}k_{\rho}k_{\alpha}k_{\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+c^{\prime}G_{\mu\gamma}k_{\nu}k_{\rho}k_{\alpha}k_{\beta}+c\,G_{\nu\rho}k_{\mu}k_{\alpha}k_{\beta}k_{\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+c^{\prime}\,G_{\nu\alpha}k_{\mu}k_{\rho}k_{\beta}k_{\gamma}+c^{\prime}G_{\nu\beta}k_{\mu}k_{\rho}k_{\alpha}k_{\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+c^{\prime}\,G_{\nu\gamma}k_{\mu}k_{\rho}k_{\alpha}k_{\beta}+c^{\prime}\,G_{\rho\alpha}k_{\mu}k_{\nu}k_{\beta}k_{\gamma}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+c^{\prime}G_{\rho\beta}k_{\mu}k_{\nu}k_{\alpha}k_{\gamma}+c^{\prime}\,G_{\rho\gamma}k_{\mu}k_{\nu}k_{\alpha}k_{\beta}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+c\,G_{\alpha\beta}k_{\mu}k_{\nu}k_{\rho}k_{\gamma}+c\,G_{\alpha\gamma}k_{\mu}k_{\nu}k_{\rho}k_{\beta}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+c\,G_{\beta\gamma}k_{\mu}k_{\nu}k_{\rho}k_{\alpha}+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+d\,k_{\mu}k_{\nu}k_{\rho}k_{\alpha}k_{\beta}k_{\gamma}\,.\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

To determine the remaining eight constants, we use Eqs. (
194
) and (
195
). We note that

\displaystyle k^{\mu}G_{\mu\nu}=0

(202)

and contract the last expression with
k^{\mu}k^{\nu}k^{\rho}k^{\alpha}
,

\displaystyle 0=\,

\displaystyle k^{\mu}k^{\nu}k^{\rho}k^{\alpha}\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho}(\lambda,\vec{k})\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\lambda,\vec{k})=

(203)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle(k^{2})^{4}\,(c\,G_{\beta\gamma}+d\,k_{\beta}k_{\gamma})\,.\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

The only solution to this equation is
c=d=0
, which eliminates two of the remaining constants. In complete analogy the contraction with
k^{\mu}k^{\nu}k^{\alpha}k^{\beta}
leads to

\displaystyle 0=\,

\displaystyle k^{\mu}k^{\nu}k^{\alpha}k^{\beta}\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho}(\lambda,\vec{k})\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\lambda,\vec{k})=

(204)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle(k^{2})^{4}\,c^{\prime}\,G_{\rho\gamma}\,,\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

which sets
c^{\prime}=0
. Next, we contract with
k^{\mu}k^{\nu}\eta^{\rho\alpha}\eta^{\beta\gamma}
and find

\displaystyle 0=\,

\displaystyle k^{\mu}k^{\nu}\eta^{\rho\alpha}\eta^{\beta\gamma}\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho}(\lambda,\vec{k})\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\lambda,\vec{k})=

(205)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle(k^{2})^{2}\,b\,(\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}_{{{\mu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\nu}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{G^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.0092 \displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle 15\,b\,(k^{2})^{2}\,,\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

which states that also
b=0
. To eliminate
b^{\prime}
and
\tilde{b}
, we have to contract the whole expression with
k^{\mu}k^{\alpha}\eta^{\nu\beta}\eta^{\rho\gamma}
,

\displaystyle 0=\,

\displaystyle k^{\mu}k^{\alpha}\eta^{\nu\beta}\eta^{\rho\gamma}\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho}(\lambda,\vec{k})\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\lambda,\vec{k})=

(206)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle(k^{2})^{2}\,(3\,\tilde{b}+12\,b^{\prime})\,,\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

and with
k^{\mu}k^{\alpha}\eta^{\nu\rho}\eta^{\beta\gamma}
,

\displaystyle 0=\,

\displaystyle k^{\mu}k^{\alpha}\eta^{\nu\rho}\eta^{\beta\gamma}\sum_{\lambda=1}^{7}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho}(\lambda,\vec{k})\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\lambda,\vec{k})=

(207)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle(k^{2})^{2}\,(9\,\tilde{b}+6\,b^{\prime})\,.\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=1}^{7}}

Thus, we conclude
b^{\prime}=\tilde{b}=0
.
Before we calculate the relation between the coefficients
a
and
a^{\prime}
, we provide the remainder expression for the completeness relation.

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda=1}^{7}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho}(\lambda,\vec{k})\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\lambda,\vec{k})=\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

(208)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle+a\,G_{\mu\nu}G_{\rho\alpha}G_{\beta\gamma}+a\,G_{\mu\nu}G_{\rho\beta}G_{\alpha\gamma}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+a\,G_{\mu\nu}G_{\rho\gamma}G_{\alpha\beta}+a\,G_{\mu\rho}G_{\nu\alpha}G_{\beta\gamma}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+a\,G_{\mu\rho}G_{\nu\beta}G_{\alpha\gamma}+a\,G_{\mu\rho}G_{\nu\gamma}G_{\alpha\beta}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+a\,G_{\mu\alpha}G_{\nu\rho}G_{\beta\gamma}+a^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\alpha}G_{\nu\beta}G_{\rho\gamma}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+a^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\alpha}G_{\nu\gamma}G_{\rho\beta}+a\,G_{\mu\beta}G_{\nu\rho}G_{\alpha\gamma}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+a^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\beta}G_{\nu\alpha}G_{\rho\gamma}+a^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\beta}G_{\nu\gamma}G_{\rho\alpha}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+a\,G_{\mu\gamma}G_{\nu\rho}G_{\alpha\beta}+a^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\gamma}G_{\nu\alpha}G_{\rho\beta}+\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

\displaystyle+a^{\prime}\,G_{\mu\gamma}G_{\nu\beta}G_{\rho\alpha}\,.\vphantom{\Bigg(\Bigg)}

The easiest way to eliminate
a^{\prime}
in favor of
a
is, to contract this expression with
\eta^{\mu\nu}\eta^{\rho\alpha}\eta^{\beta\gamma}
.

\displaystyle 0=\,

\displaystyle\eta^{\mu\nu}\eta^{\rho\alpha}\eta^{\beta\gamma}\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho}(\lambda,\vec{k})\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\lambda,\vec{k})=

(209)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle 75\,a+30\,a^{\prime}\,.\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

Thus,
a^{\prime}=-\frac{5}{2}a
. Reordering the completeness relation gives

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho}(\lambda,\,\vec{k})\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\lambda,\,\vec{k})=\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

(210)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle a\,\big\{G_{\mu\nu}\,(G_{\rho\alpha}\,G_{\beta\gamma}+G_{\rho\beta}\,G_{\alpha\gamma}+G_{\rho\gamma}\,G_{\alpha\beta})+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+G_{\mu\rho}\,(G_{\nu\alpha}\,G_{\beta\gamma}+G_{\nu\beta}\,G_{\alpha\gamma}+G_{\nu\gamma}\,G_{\alpha\beta})+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+G_{\nu\rho}\,(G_{\mu\alpha}\,G_{\beta\gamma}+G_{\mu\beta}\,G_{\alpha\gamma}+G_{\mu\gamma}\,G_{\alpha\beta})-\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{5}{2}\,\big[G_{\mu\alpha}\,(G_{\nu\beta}\,G_{\rho\gamma}+G_{\nu\gamma}\,G_{\rho\beta})+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+G_{\mu\beta}\,(G_{\nu\alpha}\,G_{\rho\gamma}+G_{\nu\gamma}\,G_{\rho\alpha})+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+G_{\mu\gamma}\,(G_{\nu\alpha}\,G_{\rho\beta}+G_{\nu\beta}\,G_{\rho\alpha})\big]\big\}\,.\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

The last constant
a
can be chosen arbitrarily. Nevertheless it might be convenient to orthonormalize the polarization tensors,

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho}(\lambda,\vec{k})\,\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho}(\lambda^{\prime},\vec{k})=-\delta_{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}\,,

(211)

which is the higher-spin equivalent to the orthonormality condition Eq. (
181
). Then it directly follows that

\displaystyle-7=\,

\displaystyle-\sum_{\lambda-3}^{+3}\delta_{\lambda\lambda}=

(212)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho}(\lambda,\vec{k})\,\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho}(\lambda,\vec{k})=-105\,a\,.

The final result is.

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho}(\lambda,\vec{k})\,\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma}(\lambda,\vec{k})=\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

(213)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{15}\,\big[G_{\mu\nu}\,(G_{\rho\alpha}\,G_{\beta\gamma}+G_{\rho\beta}\,G_{\alpha\gamma}+G_{\rho\gamma}\,G_{\alpha\beta})+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+G_{\mu\rho}\,(G_{\nu\alpha}\,G_{\beta\gamma}+G_{\nu\beta}\,G_{\alpha\gamma}+G_{\nu\gamma}\,G_{\alpha\beta})+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+G_{\nu\rho}\,(G_{\mu\alpha}\,G_{\beta\gamma}+G_{\mu\beta}\,G_{\alpha\gamma}+G_{\mu\gamma}\,G_{\alpha\beta})\big]-\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{6}\,\big[G_{\mu\alpha}\,(G_{\nu\beta}\,G_{\rho\gamma}+G_{\nu\gamma}\,G_{\rho\beta})+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+G_{\mu\beta}\,(G_{\nu\alpha}\,G_{\rho\gamma}+G_{\nu\gamma}\,G_{\rho\alpha})+\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

\displaystyle+G_{\mu\gamma}\,(G_{\nu\alpha}\,G_{\rho\beta}+G_{\nu\beta}\,G_{\rho\alpha})\big]\,.\vphantom{\sum_{\lambda=-3}^{+3}}

E.4
Spin-
J
tensor degrees of freedom

As a last step, we recapitulate for the sake of completeness, how to determine the number of degrees of freedom for a massive bosonic field (see Ref.
Fierz 1939
;
Fierz and Pauli 1939
for the original discussion). A Lorentz tensor
\phi^{...\mu\nu...}
of arbitrary rank
J
in four-dimensional Minkowski space has
4^{J}
degrees of freedom. The KG equation (
183
) does not reduce the degrees of freedom, whereas the symmetry condition (
184
) does. A combinatorial analysis is necessary and we use for simplicity an urn model: for each index of the rank-
J
tensor components there are four possible entries, which is equivalent to four different balls in the urn. The tensor field is symmetric in all indices, which means that the order of drawings does not matter. Indices can also be “drawn” several times. This leads to

\displaystyle\frac{(4-1+J)!}{J!\,(4-1)!}=\binom{4-1+J}{4-1}

(214)

possibilities, or better degrees of freedom. The next reduction is done using the traceless condition (
185
) which leads to

\displaystyle\binom{4-1+(J-2)}{4-1}

(215)

additional constraints [this is exactly the number of degrees of freedom of a symmetric tensor of rank
(J-2)
]. The last constraint, also refereed to as Lorentz constraint or transversality constraint (
186
), leads to restrictions:

\displaystyle 0=\partial_{\mu}\phi^{\cdots\mu\nu\cdots}\,.

(216)

Now it is easy to conclude, that the number of these constraints are exactly the degrees of freedom of a rank
(J-1)
symmetric traceless tensor which are:

\displaystyle\binom{4-1+(J-1)}{4-1}-\binom{4-1+(J-3)}{4-1}\,.

(217)

In total, the Fierz-Pauli equations lead to

\displaystyle\binom{4-1+J}{4-1}-\binom{4-1+(J-2)}{4-1}-\left[\binom{4-1+(J-1)}{4-1}-\binom{4-1+(J-3)}{4-1}\right]=2J+1\,.

(218)

degrees of freedom for a rank-
J
massive tensor field.

On the other hand, we know that a massive particle of total spin-
J
has also
2J+1
degrees of freedom, which correspond to the
2J+1
possible orientations of the particles spin in a magnetic field. Furthermore, one could study the behavior of particles of spin-
J
under rotations or Lorentz transformations, which would also emphasize the use massive rank-
J
tensor fields to describe those spin-
J
particles.
